## Corrections and Conditional Release Act {#corrections-and-conditional-release-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 1992, c. 20
:::

Assented to 1992-06-18

An Act respecting corrections and the conditional release and detention
of offenders and to establish the office of Correctional Investigator
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: Enacts
Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate and House
of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-105672 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6).

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Institutional and Community Corrections]{.HTitleText1} {#h-105675 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105676 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this
  Part,

  [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Commissioner of
      Corrections appointed pursuant to subsection
      6(1); ([commissaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[contraband]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[contraband]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an intoxicant,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a weapon or a component thereof, ammunition for
        a weapon, and anything that is designed to kill, injure or
        disable a person or that is altered so as to be capable of
        killing, injuring or disabling a person, when possessed without
        prior authorization,

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an explosive or a bomb or a component thereof,

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} currency over any applicable prescribed limit,
        when possessed without prior authorization, and

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any item not described in paragraphs (a) to (d)
        that could jeopardize the security of a penitentiary or the
        safety of persons, when that item is possessed without prior
        authorization; ([objets interdits]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[day parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[day parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      II; ([semi-liberté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Indigenous]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Indigenous]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a person,
      includes a First Nation person, an Inuit or a Métis
      person; ([autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[inmate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[inmate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who is in a penitentiary pursuant to

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sentence, committal or transfer to
          penitentiary, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a condition imposed by the Parole Board of
          Canada in connection with day parole or statutory release, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who, having been sentenced, committed
        or transferred to penitentiary,

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is temporarily outside penitentiary by reason
          of a temporary absence or work release authorized under this
          Act, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is temporarily outside penitentiary for
          reasons other than a temporary absence, work release, parole
          or statutory release, but is under the direction or
          supervision of a staff member or of a person authorized by the
          Service; ([détenu]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[institutional head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[institutional head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to a
      penitentiary, means the person who is normally in charge of the
      penitentiary; ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[intoxicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[intoxicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance that, if
      taken into the body, has the potential to impair or alter
      judgment, behaviour or the capacity to recognize reality or meet
      the ordinary demands of life, but does not include caffeine,
      nicotine or any authorized medication used in accordance with
      directions given by a staff member or a registered health care
      professional; ([substance intoxicante]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means long-term
      supervision ordered under subsection 753(4), 753.01(5) or (6) or
      753.1(3) or subparagraph 759(3)(a)(i) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([surveillance de longue
      durée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[mental health assessment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[mental health assessment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
      assessment of the mental health of a person conducted by a medical
      professional with recognized specialty training in mental health
      diagnosis and treatment, such as a psychiatrist, psychologist or
      psychiatric nurse or a primary care physician who has had
      psychiatric training. ([évaluation de la santé
      mentale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of Public
      Safety and Emergency Preparedness; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an inmate, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who, having been sentenced, committed
        or transferred to penitentiary, is outside penitentiary

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by reason of parole or statutory release,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to an agreement referred to in
          subsection 81(1), or

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to a court
          order; ([délinquant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      II; ([libération conditionnelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a facility of any description, including all
        lands connected therewith, that is operated, permanently or
        temporarily, by the Service for the care and custody of inmates,
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any place declared to be a penitentiary
        pursuant to section 7; ([pénitencier]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by
      regulation; ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in Part II; ([commission provinciale]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a sentence of imprisonment
      and includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a sentence imposed by a foreign entity on a
        Canadian offender who has been transferred to Canada under the
        [International Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6), and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a youth sentence imposed under the [Youth
        Criminal Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) consisting of a custodial
        portion and a portion to be served under supervision in the
        community subject to conditions under paragraph 42(2)(n) of that
        Act or under conditional supervision under paragraph
        42(2)(o), (q) or (r) of that Act; ([peine]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"} ou [peine d'emprisonnement]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[Service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Correctional Service of
      Canada described in section 5; ([Service]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[staff member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[staff member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee of the
      Service; ([agent]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in Part II; ([libération d'office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[unescorted temporary absence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unescorted temporary absence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in Part II; ([permission de sortir sans
      escorte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of an offence, means an
      individual who has suffered physical or emotional harm, property
      damage or economic loss as the result of the commission of the
      offence; ([victime]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[visitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[visitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person other than an
      inmate or a staff member; ([visiteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a day on which offices
      of the federal public administration are generally open in the
      province in question. ([jour ouvrable]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by this Part or by
  regulations made under paragraph 96(b),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} powers, duties and functions that this Part assigns
    to the Commissioner may only be exercised or performed by the
    Commissioner or, where the Commissioner is absent or incapacitated
    or where the office is vacant, by the person acting in the place of
    the Commissioner; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} powers, duties and functions that this Part assigns
    to the institutional head may only be exercised or performed by the
    institutional head or, where the institutional head is absent or
    incapacitated or where the office is vacant, by the person who, at
    the relevant time, is in charge of the penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting on victim's behalf

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, any of the following
  individuals may act on the victim's behalf if the victim is dead or
  incapable of acting on their own behalf:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the victim's spouse, or if the victim is dead,
    their spouse at the time of death;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the individual who is or was at the time of the
    victim's death, cohabiting with them in a conjugal relationship,
    having so cohabited for a period of at least one year;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a relative or a dependant of the victim;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an individual who has in law or fact custody, or is
    responsible for the care or support, of the victim; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an individual who has in law or fact custody, or is
    responsible for the care or support, of a dependant of the victim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, an individual is not a
  victim, or entitled to act on a victim's behalf, in relation to an
  offence, if the individual is the offender.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to persons subject to
long-term supervision order

**[[2.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2.1 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
required to be supervised by a long-term supervision order is deemed to
be an offender for the purposes of this Part, and sections 3, 4, 23 to
27, 55 and 56, subsections 57(2) and 66(3), sections 68, 69, 76, 77 and
79 to 82, paragraph 87(b) and sections 90 and 91 apply, with such
modifications as the circumstances require, to the person and to the
long-term supervision of that person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 17, s. 12
:::

### [Purpose and Principles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105740 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of correctional system

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of the federal
correctional system is to contribute to the maintenance of a just,
peaceful and safe society by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} carrying out sentences imposed by courts through the
  safe and humane custody and supervision of offenders; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} assisting the rehabilitation of offenders and their
  reintegration into the community as law-abiding citizens through the
  provision of programs in penitentiaries and in the community.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paramount consideration

**[[3.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.1 .sectionLabel}** The protection of
society is the paramount consideration for the Service in the
corrections process.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 54
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Principles that guide Service

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** The principles that guide
the Service in achieving the purpose referred to in section 3 are as
follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sentence is carried out having regard to all
  relevant available information, including the stated reasons and
  recommendations of the sentencing judge, the nature and gravity of the
  offence, the degree of responsibility of the offender, information
  from the trial or sentencing process, the release policies of and
  comments from the Parole Board of Canada and information obtained from
  victims, offenders and other components of the criminal justice
  system;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Service enhances its effectiveness and openness
  through the timely exchange of relevant information with victims,
  offenders and other components of the criminal justice system and
  through communication about its correctional policies and programs to
  victims, offenders and the public;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Service uses the least restrictive measures
  consistent with the protection of society, staff members and
  offenders;

- [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the Service considers alternatives to custody in a
  penitentiary, including the alternatives referred to in sections 29
  and 81;

- [(c.2)]{.lawlabel} the Service ensures the effective delivery of
  programs to offenders, including correctional, educational, vocational
  training and volunteer programs, with a view to improving access to
  alternatives to custody in a penitentiary and to promoting
  rehabilitation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} offenders retain the rights of all members of society
  except those that are, as a consequence of the sentence, lawfully and
  necessarily removed or restricted;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the Service facilitates the involvement of members of
  the public in matters relating to the operations of the Service;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} correctional decisions are made in a forthright and
  fair manner, with access by the offender to an effective grievance
  procedure;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} correctional policies, programs and practices respect
  gender, ethnic, cultural, religious and linguistic differences, sexual
  orientation and gender identity and expression, and are responsive to
  the special needs of women, Indigenous persons, visible minorities,
  persons requiring mental health care and other groups;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} offenders are expected to obey penitentiary rules and
  conditions governing temporary absences, work release, parole,
  statutory release and long-term supervision and to actively
  participate in meeting the objectives of their correctional plans,
  including by participating in programs designed to promote their
  rehabilitation and reintegration; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} staff members are properly selected and trained and
  are given

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} appropriate career development opportunities,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} good working conditions, including a workplace
    environment that is free of practices that undermine a person's
    sense of personal dignity, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} opportunities to participate in the development
    of correctional policies and programs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 4
- 1995, c. 42, s. 2(F)
- 2012, c. 1, ss. 54, 160
- [2019, c. 27, s. 2](#1151483-1170943){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151483-1170943.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20130228.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Correctional Service of Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105765 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correctional Service of Canada

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** There shall continue to be
a correctional service in and for Canada, to be known as the
Correctional Service of Canada, which shall be responsible for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the care and custody of inmates;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provision of programs that contribute to the
  rehabilitation of offenders and to their successful reintegration into
  the community;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the preparation of inmates for release;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} parole, statutory release supervision and long-term
  supervision of offenders;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} maintaining a program of public education about the
  operations of the Service; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of arrangements entered into
  between the Commissioner and the Canada Border Services Agency under
  section 94.3.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 5
- 1997, c. 17, s. 13
- [2024, c. 17, s. 385](#1468453-1479808){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468453-1479808.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may appoint a person to be known as the
  Commissioner of Corrections who, under the direction of the Minister,
  has the control and management of the Service and all matters
  connected with the Service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National headquarters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The national headquarters of the Service and the
  offices of the Commissioner shall be in the National Capital Region
  described in the schedule to the [National Capital
  Act](/eng/acts/N-4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regional headquarters

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may establish regional headquarters
  of the Service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penitentiaries

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (3), the Commissioner may, by order, declare any prison
  as defined in the [Prisons and Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20), or
  any hospital, to be a penitentiary in respect of any person or class
  of persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the Governor in Council
  may, by order, declare any place to be a penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No prison, hospital or place administered or
  supervised under the authority of an Act of the legislature of a
  province may be declared a penitentiary under subsection (1) or (2)
  without the approval of an officer designated by the lieutenant
  governor of that province.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lands constituting penitentiary

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** In any proceedings before a
court in Canada in which a question arises concerning the location or
description of lands alleged to constitute a penitentiary, a certificate
purporting to be signed by the Commissioner, setting out the location or
description of those lands as constituting a penitentiary, is admissible
in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the contrary, is
proof that the lands as located or described in the certificate
constitute a penitentiary.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lawful custody

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty, a
person who is an inmate by virtue of subparagraph (b)(ii) of the
definition "inmate" in section 2 shall be deemed to be in the lawful
custody of the Service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Peace officer status

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may in
writing designate any staff member, either by name or by class, to be a
peace officer, and a staff member so designated has all the powers,
authority, protection and privileges that a peace officer has by law in
respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offender subject to a warrant or to an order for
  long-term supervision; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person, while the person is in a penitentiary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 10
- 1995, c. 42, s. 3
- 1997, c. 17, s. 14
:::

### [Reception of Inmates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105799 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
sentenced, committed or transferred to penitentiary may be received into
any penitentiary, and any designation of a particular penitentiary in
the warrant of committal is of no force or effect.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommitment to custody

**[[11.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.1 .sectionLabel}** Where a person who is
sentenced, committed or transferred to penitentiary is at large without
lawful authority before the expiration of the sentence according to law
and where no alternative means of arrest are available, the
institutional head may, by warrant, authorize the apprehension and
recommitment of the person to custody in a penitentiary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 42, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fifteen day delay

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** In order to better enable
a person who has been sentenced to penitentiary or who is required by
law to be transferred to penitentiary to file an appeal or attend to
personal affairs, such a person shall not be received in penitentiary
until the expiration of fifteen days after the day on which the person
was sentenced, unless the person agrees to be transferred to a
penitentiary before the expiration of those fifteen days.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical certificate

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** The institutional head
may refuse to receive a person referred to in section 12 into the
penitentiary if there is not a certificate signed by a registered health
care professional setting out available health information and stating
whether or not the person appears to be suffering from a dangerous,
infectious or contagious disease.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confinement in provincial facility

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who, by virtue of section 12 or 13, is not received into a
  penitentiary shall be confined in a provincial correctional facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person in charge of the provincial correctional
  facility to whom a person referred to in subsection (1) is delivered
  shall, on being presented with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the warrant of committal to penitentiary, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the warrant of committal certified by any
    judge of a superior or provincial court, by any justice of the
    peace, or by the clerk of the court in which the person was
    convicted,

  confine the person in the provincial correctional facility until the
  person is transferred to penitentiary or released from custody in
  accordance with law.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 14
- 1995, c. 42, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Newfoundland and Labrador

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any requirement in
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or under the [Youth Criminal
  Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) that a person be sentenced, committed or
  transferred to penitentiary, such a person in the Province of
  Newfoundland and Labrador shall not be received in a penitentiary
  without the approval of an officer designated by the Lieutenant
  Governor of Newfoundland and Labrador.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who, pursuant to subsection (1), is not
  received in a penitentiary shall be confined in the provincial
  correctional facility in Newfoundland and Labrador known as *Her
  Majesty's Penitentiary*, and is subject to all the statutes,
  regulations and rules applicable in that facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement re cost

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, with the approval of the Governor
  in Council, enter into an agreement with the Province of Newfoundland
  and Labrador providing for the payment to the Province of the cost of
  maintaining persons who are confined pursuant to subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 15
- 2002, c. 1, s. 172
- 2015, c. 3, s. 172
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20030401.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Correctional Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105829 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives for offender's behaviour

- **[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall cause a
  correctional plan to be developed in consultation with the offender as
  soon as practicable after their reception in a penitentiary. The plan
  is to contain, among others, the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the level of intervention in respect of the
    offender's needs; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} objectives for

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the offender's behaviour, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} to conduct themselves in a manner that
        demonstrates respect for other persons and property,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} to obey penitentiary rules and respect the
        conditions governing their conditional release, if any,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} their participation in programs, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the meeting of their court-ordered obligations,
      including restitution to victims or child support.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance of plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The plan is to be maintained in consultation with the
  offender in order to ensure that they receive the most effective
  programs at the appropriate time in their sentence to rehabilitate
  them and prepare them for reintegration into the community, on
  release, as a law-abiding citizen.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mental health assessment

  [(2.01)]{.lawlabel} In order to ensure that the plan can be developed
  in a manner that takes any mental health needs of the offender into
  consideration, the institutional head shall, as soon as practicable
  after the day on which the offender is received but not later than the
  30th day after that day, refer the offender's case to the portion of
  the Service that administers health care for the purpose of conducting
  a mental health assessment of the offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Update of plan --- structured
  intervention unit

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If an offender is in a structured intervention unit
  and a determination is made under subsection 29.01(2), paragraph
  37.3(1)(b) or section 37.4 or 37.8 that the offender should remain in
  the structured intervention unit, the institutional head shall, as
  soon as practicable after the determination, cause the offender's
  correctional plan to be updated, in consultation with the offender, in
  order to ensure that they receive the most effective programs at the
  appropriate time during their confinement in the structured
  intervention unit and to prepare them for reintegration into the
  mainstream inmate population as soon as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progress towards meeting objectives

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In making decisions on program selection for --- or
  the transfer or conditional release of --- an inmate, the Service
  shall take into account the offender's progress towards meeting the
  objectives of their correctional plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 55
- [2019, c. 27, s. 3](#1151490-1205729){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151490-1205729.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.1-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 15.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incentive measures

**[[15.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.2 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
provide offenders with incentives to encourage them to make progress
towards meeting the objectives of their correctional plans.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 55
:::

### [Exchange of Service Agreements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105850 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements with provinces

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, with the approval of the Governor in Council, enter into
  an agreement with the government of a province for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the confinement in provincial correctional
    facilities or hospitals in that province of persons sentenced,
    committed or transferred to penitentiary; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the confinement in penitentiary of persons
    sentenced or committed to imprisonment for less than two years for
    offences under any Act of Parliament or any regulations made
    thereunder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of confinement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a person who is confined
  in a penitentiary pursuant to an agreement entered into under
  paragraph (1)(b) is, despite section 743.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subject to all the statutes, regulations and
  rules applicable in the penitentiary in which the person is confined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release date

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The release date of an offender who is transferred to
  penitentiary pursuant to an agreement entered into under paragraph
  (1)(b) shall be determined by crediting against the sentence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remission, statutory or earned, standing to the
    offender's credit on the day of the transfer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum remission that could have been earned
    on the balance of the sentence pursuant to the [Prisons and
    Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 16
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, s. 6
- 2012, c. 1, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Escorted Temporary Absences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105864 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary absences may be authorized

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  institutional head may, subject to section 746.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 140.3(2) of the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against
  Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), authorize the
  temporary absence of an inmate, other than an inmate described in
  subsection 17.1(1), if the inmate is escorted by a staff member or
  other person authorized by the institutional head and, in the opinion
  of the institutional head,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inmate will not, by reoffending, present an
    undue risk to society during an absence authorized under this
    section;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is desirable for the inmate to be absent from
    the penitentiary for medical or administrative reasons, community
    service, family contact, including parental responsibilities,
    personal development for rehabilitative purposes or compassionate
    reasons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the inmate's behaviour while under sentence does
    not preclude authorizing the absence; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a structured plan for the absence has been
    prepared.

  The temporary absence may be for an unlimited period if it is
  authorized for medical reasons or for a period of not more than five
  days or, with the Commissioner's approval, for a period of more than
  five days but not more than 15 days if it is authorized for reasons
  other than medical reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head may impose, in relation to a
  temporary absence, any conditions that the institutional head
  considers reasonable and necessary in order to protect society.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head may cancel a temporary absence
  either before or after its commencement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons to be given

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall give the inmate written
  reasons for the authorizing, refusal or cancellation of a temporary
  absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel time

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the period authorized for the purposes
  of a temporary absence, an inmate may be granted the time necessary to
  travel to and from the place where the absence is authorized to be
  spent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation to provincial hospital

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to an agreement under paragraph
  16(1)(a), an inmate has been admitted to a hospital operated by a
  provincial government in which the liberty of patients is normally
  subject to restrictions, the institutional head may confer on the
  person in charge of the hospital, for such period and subject to such
  conditions as the institutional head specifies, any of the
  institutional head's powers under this section in relation to that
  inmate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 17
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, s. 7(F)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 108
- 2000, c. 24, s. 34
- 2013, c. 24, s. 127
- 2014, c. 36, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary absences may be approved ---
exception

- **[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Parole Board of Canada may
  authorize the temporary absence of an inmate who is serving a sentence
  of imprisonment for life imposed as a minimum punishment and is
  eligible for day parole if the inmate is escorted by a staff member or
  other person authorized by the institutional head and the Parole Board
  of Canada is of the opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inmate will not, by reoffending, present an
    undue risk to society during an absence authorized under this
    section;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is desirable for the inmate to be absent from
    the penitentiary for administrative reasons, community service,
    family contact, including parental responsibilities, personal
    development for rehabilitative purposes or compassionate reasons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the inmate's behaviour while under sentence does
    not preclude authorizing the absence; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a structured plan for the absence has been
    prepared.

  The temporary absence may be for a period of not more than 15 days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent temporary absence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Parole Board of Canada authorizes the
  temporary absence of an inmate under subsection (1) for community
  service, family contact, including parental responsibilities, or
  personal development for rehabilitative purposes and the temporary
  absence is not cancelled because the inmate has breached a condition,
  the institutional head may authorize that inmate's subsequent
  temporary absences with escort if the institutional head is of the
  opinion that the criteria set out in paragraphs (1)(a) to (d) are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent temporary absence --- Parole
  Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a temporary absence authorized by the
  institutional head is cancelled because the inmate breached a
  condition in relation to it, the inmate's subsequent temporary absence
  may be authorized only by the Parole Board of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Parole Board of Canada or the institutional head,
  as the case may be, may impose, in relation to a temporary absence
  that it authorizes, any condition that it considers reasonable and
  necessary in order to protect society.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation and reasons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head may cancel a temporary absence
  that is authorized under this section either before or after its
  commencement and shall give the inmate written reasons for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization or refusal and reasons

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Parole Board of Canada or the institutional head,
  as the case may be, shall give the inmate written reasons for
  authorizing or refusing a temporary absence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 36, s. 1.1
:::

### [Work Releases]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105905 .Subheading}

Definition of [[work release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[work release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a structured
  program of release of specified duration for work or community service
  outside the penitentiary, under the supervision of a staff member or
  other person or organization authorized by the institutional head.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work releases may be authorized

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an inmate is eligible for unescorted temporary
  absences under Part II or pursuant to section 746.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5) or subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against
  Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), and, in the opinion of
  the institutional head,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inmate will not, by reoffending, present an
    undue risk to society during a work release,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is desirable for the inmate to participate in a
    structured program of work or community service in the community,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the inmate's behaviour while under sentence does
    not preclude authorizing the work release, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a structured plan for the work release has been
    prepared,

  the institutional head may authorize a work release, for such duration
  as is fixed by the institutional head, subject to the approval of the
  Commissioner if the duration is to exceed sixty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head may impose, in relation to a
  work release, any conditions that the institutional head considers
  reasonable and necessary in order to protect society.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension or cancellation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head may suspend or cancel a work
  release either before or after its commencement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons to be given

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall give the inmate written
  reasons for the authorizing, refusal, suspension or cancellation of a
  work release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a work release is suspended or cancelled after
  its commencement, the institutional head may cause a warrant in
  writing to be issued authorizing the apprehension and recommitment to
  custody of the inmate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 18
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, ss. 8, 71(F)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 109
- 2000, c. 24, s. 35
- 2013, c. 24, s. 127
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 18"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Investigations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105926 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  an inmate dies or suffers serious bodily injury, the Service shall,
  whether or not there is an investigation under section 20, forthwith
  investigate the matter and report thereon to the Commissioner or to a
  person designated by the Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a death that results from an inmate receiving
    [[medical assistance in dying]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
    section 241.1 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in accordance
    with section 241.2 of that Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a registered health care professional advises
    the Service in writing that the registered health care professional
    has reasonable grounds to believe that an inmate's death is from a
    natural cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Correctional Investigator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall give the Correctional Investigator,
  as defined in Part III, a copy of its report referred to in subsection
  (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 19
- 2016, c. 3, s. 8
- [2019, c. 27, s. 4](#1151492-1170948){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151492-1170948.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20160617.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quality of care review

- **[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a registered health care professional advises the Service in writing
  that the registered health care professional has reasonable grounds to
  believe that the death of an inmate is from a natural cause, the
  Service shall, whether or not there is an investigation under section
  20, without delay, cause a review to be conducted by a registered
  health care professional employed or engaged by the Service for the
  purpose of determining the quality of care provided to the inmate in
  the penitentiary. The registered health care professional shall report
  on the review to the Commissioner or to a person designated by the
  Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Correctional Investigator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall give the [[Correctional
  Investigator]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in Part III, a copy
  of its report referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 5](#1151499-1170955){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151499-1170955.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special investigations

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
appoint a person or persons to investigate and report on any matter
relating to the operations of the Service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of [Inquiries
Act](/eng/acts/I-11)

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** Sections 7 to 13 of the
[Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11) apply in respect of investigations
carried on under section 20

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} as if the references to "commissioners" in those
  sections were references to the person or persons appointed under
  section 20; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} with such other modifications as the circumstances
  require.

### [Compensation for Death or Disability]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105942 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may pay compensation

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The Minister or a person
authorized by the Minister may, subject to and in accordance with the
regulations, pay compensation in respect of the death or disability of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an inmate, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person on day parole

that is attributable to the participation of that inmate or person in an
approved program.

### [Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-105948 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to obtain certain information
about offender

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a
  person is sentenced, committed or transferred to penitentiary, the
  Service shall take all reasonable steps to obtain, as soon as is
  practicable,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} relevant information about the offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} relevant information about the person's personal
    history, including the person's social, economic, criminal and
    young-offender history;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any reasons and recommendations relating to the
    sentencing or committal that are given or made by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the court that convicts, sentences or commits the
      person, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any court that hears an appeal from the
      conviction, sentence or committal;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any reports relevant to the conviction, sentence or
    committal that are submitted to a court mentioned in subparagraph
    (c)(i) or (ii); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other information relevant to administering the
    sentence or committal, including existing information from the
    victim, the victim impact statement and the transcript of any
    comments made by the sentencing judge regarding parole eligibility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access by offender

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where access to the information obtained by the
  Service pursuant to subsection (1) is requested by the offender in
  writing, the offender shall be provided with access in the prescribed
  manner to such information as would be disclosed under the [Privacy
  Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and the [Access to Information
  Act](/eng/acts/A-1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure to Service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No provision in the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21) or
  the [Access to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1) shall operate so as to
  limit or prevent the Service from obtaining any information referred
  to in paragraphs (1)(a) to (e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accuracy, etc., of information

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall take all reasonable steps to ensure that any information
  about an offender that it uses is as accurate, up to date and complete
  as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender who has been given access to
  information by the Service pursuant to subsection 23(2) believes that
  there is an error or omission therein,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender may request the Service to correct
    that information; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the request is refused, the Service shall
    attach to the information a notation indicating that the offender
    has requested a correction and setting out the correction requested.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 24
- 1995, c. 42, s. 9(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to give information to parole
boards, etc.

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall give, at the appropriate times, to the Parole Board of
  Canada, provincial governments, provincial parole boards, police, and
  any body authorized by the Service to supervise offenders, all
  information under its control that is relevant to release
  decision-making or to the supervision or surveillance of offenders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Police to be notified of releases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before the release of an inmate on an unescorted
  temporary absence, parole or statutory release, the Service shall
  notify all police forces that have jurisdiction at the destination of
  the inmate if that destination is known.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to give information to police
  in some cases

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Service has reasonable grounds to believe
  that an inmate who is about to be released by reason of the expiration
  of the sentence will, on release, pose a threat to any person, the
  Service shall, prior to the release and on a timely basis, take all
  reasonable steps to give the police all information under its control
  that is relevant to that perceived threat.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 25
- 1995, c. 42, s. 71(F)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information to victims

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At the
  request of a victim of an offence committed by an offender, the
  Commissioner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall disclose to the victim the following
    information about the offender:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the offender's name,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the offence of which the offender was convicted
      and the court that convicted the offender,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date of commencement and length of the
      sentence that the offender is serving, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} eligibility dates and review dates applicable to
      the offender under this Act in respect of temporary absences or
      parole;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may disclose to the victim any of the following
    information about the offender, where in the Commissioner's opinion
    the interest of the victim in such disclosure clearly outweighs any
    invasion of the offender's privacy that could result from the
    disclosure:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the offender's age,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the name and location of the penitentiary in
      which the sentence is being served,

    - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} if the offender is transferred, a summary of
      the reasons for the transfer and the name and location of the
      penitentiary in which the sentence is being served,

    - [(ii.2)]{.lawlabel} if the offender is to be transferred to a
      minimum security institution as designated by Commissioner's
      Directive and it is possible to notify the victim before the
      transfer, a summary of the reasons for the transfer and the name
      and location of the institution in which the sentence is to be
      served,

    - [(ii.3)]{.lawlabel} the programs that were designed to address the
      needs of the offender and contribute to their successful
      reintegration into the community in which the offender is
      participating or has participated,

    - [(ii.4)]{.lawlabel} the serious disciplinary offences that the
      offender has committed,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} information pertaining to the offender's
      correctional plan, including information regarding the offender's
      progress towards meeting the objectives of the plan,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date of any hearing for the purposes of a
      review under section 130,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} that the offender has been removed from Canada
      under the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
      Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) before the expiration of the sentence, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2015, c. 13, s. 46\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} whether the offender is in custody and, if not,
      the reason why the offender is not in custody;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall disclose to the victim any of the following
    information about the offender, if, in the Commissioner's opinion,
    the disclosure would not have a negative impact on the safety of the
    public:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date, if any, on which the offender is to be
      released on temporary absence, work release, parole or statutory
      release,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the conditions attached to the offender's
      temporary absence, work release, parole or statutory release,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the destination of the offender on any
      temporary absence, work release, parole or statutory release,
      whether the offender will be in the vicinity of the victim while
      travelling to that destination and the reasons for any temporary
      absence; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall provide the victim with access to a
    photograph of the offender taken on the occurrence of the earliest
    of any of the following --- and any subsequent photograph of the
    offender taken by the Service --- if, in the Commissioner's opinion,
    to do so would not have a negative impact on the safety of the
    public:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the release of the offender on unescorted
      temporary absence,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the offender's work release,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the offender's release on parole, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the offender's release by virtue of statutory
      release or the expiration of the sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Timing of disclosure

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall disclose the information
  referred to in paragraph (1)(c) before the day on which the offender
  is released and, unless it is not practicable to do so, the
  Commissioner shall disclose it at least 14 days before that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing duty to disclose

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall disclose to the victim any
  changes to the information referred to in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person has been transferred from a
  penitentiary to a provincial correctional facility, the Commissioner
  may, at the request of a victim of an offence committed by that
  person, disclose to the victim the name of the province in which the
  provincial correctional facility is located, if in the Commissioner's
  opinion the interest of the victim in such disclosure clearly
  outweighs any invasion of the person's privacy that could result from
  the disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information to other
  persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) also applies, with such modifications
  as the circumstances require, to a person who satisfies the
  Commissioner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person suffered physical or emotional
    harm, property damage or economic loss, as the result of an act of
    an offender, whether or not the offender was prosecuted or convicted
    for that act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that a complaint was made to the police or the
    Crown attorney, or an information was laid under the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in respect of that act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) also applies, with such modifications
  as the circumstances require, to a person who satisfies the
  Commissioner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the person suffered physical or emotional
    harm, property damage or economic loss, as the result of an act of a
    person referred to in subsection (2), whether or not the person
    referred to in subsection (2) was prosecuted or convicted for that
    act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that a complaint was made to the police or the
    Crown attorney, or an information was laid under the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in respect of that act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A victim may designate a representative to whom the
  information referred to in subsections (1) and (2) is to be disclosed
  on the victim's behalf, or to whom access to a photograph referred to
  in paragraph (1)(d) is to be provided. In that case, the victim shall
  provide the Commissioner with the representative's contact
  information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of request

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A victim who has made a request referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) may inform the Commissioner in writing that they
  no longer want the information to be disclosed to them or access to
  the photograph. In that case, the Commissioner shall not contact them,
  or their representative, if any, unless the victim subsequently makes
  the request again.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed withdrawal of request

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may consider a victim to have
  withdrawn a request referred to in subsection (1) or (2) if the
  Commissioner has made reasonable efforts to contact the victim and has
  failed to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other persons

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (5) to (7) also apply, with any necessary
  modifications, to a person who has satisfied the Commissioner of the
  matters referred to in paragraphs (3)(a) and (b) or (4)(a) and (b).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 26
- 2012, c. 1, s. 57
- 2015, c. 13, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20150723.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Victim-offender mediation services

- **[[26.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall provide every
  victim, and every person referred to in subsection 26(3), who has
  registered themselves with the Service for the purposes of this
  section with information about its restorative justice programs and
  its victim-offender mediation services, and, on the victim's or other
  person's request, may take measures to provide those services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service's victim-offender mediation services are
  to be provided in accordance with the Commissioner's Directives and
  they may be provided only with the informed consent of the
  participants that is voluntarily given.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 13, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be given to offenders

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  an offender is entitled by this Part or the regulations to make
  representations in relation to a decision to be taken by the Service
  about the offender, the person or body that is to take the decision
  shall, subject to subsection (3), give the offender, a reasonable
  period before the decision is to be taken, all the information to be
  considered in the taking of the decision or a summary of that
  information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender is entitled by this Part or the
  regulations to be given reasons for a decision taken by the Service
  about the offender, the person or body that takes the decision shall,
  subject to subsection (3), give the offender, forthwith after the
  decision is taken, all the information that was considered in the
  taking of the decision or a summary of that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except in relation to decisions on disciplinary
  offences, where the Commissioner has reasonable grounds to believe
  that disclosure of information under subsection (1) or (2) would
  jeopardize

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety of any person,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the security of a penitentiary, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of any lawful investigation,

  the Commissioner may authorize the withholding from the offender of as
  much information as is strictly necessary in order to protect the
  interest identified in paragraph (a), (b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to interpreter

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An offender who does not have an adequate
  understanding of at least one of Canada's official languages is
  entitled to the assistance of an interpreter

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at any hearing provided for by this Part or the
    regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of understanding materials
    provided to the offender pursuant to this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 27
- 1995, c. 42, s. 10(F)
:::

### [Placement and Transfer of Inmates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106058 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria for selection of penitentiary

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** If a person is or is to
be confined in a penitentiary, the Service shall take all reasonable
steps to ensure that the penitentiary in which they are confined is one
that provides them with the least restrictive environment for that
person, taking into account

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the degree and kind of custody and control necessary
  for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the safety of the public,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the safety of that person and other persons in the
    penitentiary, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the security of the penitentiary;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} accessibility to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's home community and family,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a compatible cultural environment, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a compatible linguistic environment; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the availability of appropriate programs and services
  and the person's willingness to participate in those programs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 28
- 2012, c. 1, s. 58
- [2019, c. 27, s. 6](#1151501-1170957){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151501-1170957.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfers

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
authorize the transfer of a person who is sentenced, transferred or
committed to a penitentiary

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to a hospital, including any mental health facility,
  or to a provincial correctional facility, in accordance with an
  agreement entered into under paragraph 16(1)(a) and any applicable
  regulations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} within a penitentiary, from an area that has been
  assigned a security classification under section 29.1 to another area
  that has been assigned a security classification under that section,
  in accordance with the regulations made under paragraph 96(d), subject
  to section 28; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to another penitentiary, in accordance with the
  regulations made under paragraph 96(d), subject to section 28.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 29
- 1995, c. 42, s. 11
- [2019, c. 27, s. 7](#1151508-1205791){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151508-1205791.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfers to structured intervention unit

- **[[29.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A staff member who holds a position
  lower in rank than that of institutional head and who is designated by
  the Commissioner may, in accordance with the regulations made under
  paragraph 96(g), and subject to section 28, authorize the transfer of
  a person who is sentenced, transferred or committed to a penitentiary
  into a structured intervention unit in the penitentiary or in another
  penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision --- institutional head

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall determine, in accordance
  with regulations made under paragraph 96(g), whether an inmate should
  remain in a structured intervention unit within the period of five
  working days that begins on the first working day on which the inmate
  is confined in the unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 7](#1151508-1205804){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151508-1205804.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Security Classification]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106078 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner to classify penitentiaries
or areas

**[[29.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.1 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
assign the security classification of "minimum security", "medium
security", "maximum security" or "multi-level security", or any other
prescribed security classification, to each penitentiary or to any area
in a penitentiary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 8](#1151512-1170961){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151512-1170961.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to classify each inmate

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall assign a security classification of maximum, medium or
  minimum to each inmate in accordance with the regulations made under
  paragraph 96(z.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to give reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall give each inmate reasons, in
  writing, for assigning a particular security classification or for
  changing that classification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subclassification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within the maximum and medium security
  classifications, the Commissioner may assign an inmate to a
  subclassification in accordance with the regulations made under
  paragraph 96(z.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner to give reasons

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner or the staff member designated by
  the Commissioner shall give each inmate reasons, in writing, for
  assigning them to a subclassification or for changing that
  subclassification.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 30
- 2012, c. 1, s. 59
- [2019, c. 27, s. 9(F)](#1151514-1170962){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151514-1170962.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Structured Intervention Units]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106090 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
designate a penitentiary or any area in a penitentiary to be a
structured intervention unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 31
- 2012, c. 1, s. 60
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205818){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205818.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  purpose of a structured intervention unit is to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide an appropriate living environment for an
    inmate who cannot be maintained in the mainstream inmate population
    for security or other reasons; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the inmate with an opportunity for
    meaningful human contact and an opportunity to participate in
    programs and to have access to services that respond to the inmate's
    specific needs and the risks posed by the inmate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Physical barriers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(b), every
  reasonable effort shall be made to ensure that the opportunity to
  interact through human contact is not mediated or interposed by
  physical barriers such as bars, security glass, door hatches or
  screens.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall maintain a record of every instance
  of an interaction referred to in paragraph (1)(b) that is mediated or
  interposed by such physical barriers.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 32
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1206241){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1206241.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** An inmate's confinement
in a structured intervention unit is to end as soon as possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 33
- 1995, c. 42, s. 12
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205832){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205832.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer to unit

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member may authorize the transfer of an inmate into a structured
  intervention unit under subsection 29.01(1) only if the staff member
  is satisfied that there is no reasonable alternative to the inmate's
  confinement in a structured intervention unit and the staff member
  believes on reasonable grounds that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inmate has acted, has attempted to act or
    intends to act in a manner that jeopardizes the safety of any person
    or the security of a penitentiary and allowing the inmate to be in
    the mainstream inmate population would jeopardize the safety of any
    person or the security of the penitentiary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allowing the inmate to be in the mainstream inmate
    population would jeopardize the inmate's safety; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allowing the inmate to be in the mainstream inmate
    population would interfere with an investigation that could lead to
    a criminal charge or a charge under subsection 41(2) of a serious
    disciplinary offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of transfers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall maintain a record of every instance
  in which an inmate is authorized to be transferred into a structured
  intervention unit indicating the reasons for granting the
  authorization and any alternative that was considered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No later than one working day after the day on which
  the transfer of an inmate is authorized, the Service shall, orally,
  provide the inmate with notice that the authorization was granted as
  well as the reasons for it and no later than two working days after
  the day on which the transfer of an inmate is authorized, the Service
  shall provide the inmate with those reasons in writing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 34
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1206254){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1206254.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inmate rights

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** An inmate in a structured
intervention unit has the same rights as other inmates, except for those
that cannot be exercised due to limitations specific to the structured
intervention unit or security requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 35
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1206259){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1206259.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-35-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 35"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of Service

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall, every day, between the hours of 7:00 a.m. and 10:00
  p.m., provide an inmate in a structured intervention unit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an opportunity to spend a minimum of four hours
    outside the inmate's cell; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an opportunity to interact, for a minimum of two
    hours, with others, through activities including, but not limited
    to,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} programs, interventions and services that
      encourage the inmate to make progress towards the objectives of
      their correctional plan or that support the inmate's reintegration
      into the mainstream inmate population, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} leisure time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time included

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Time spent interacting under paragraph (1)(b) outside
  an inmate's cell counts as time spent outside the inmate's cell under
  paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time not included

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an inmate takes a shower outside their cell, the
  time spent doing so does not count as time spent outside the inmate's
  cell under paragraph (1)(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 36
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1206273){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1206273.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 36(1)(a) or (b), as the
  case may be, does not apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the inmate refuses to avail themselves of the
    opportunity referred to in that paragraph;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the inmate, at the time the opportunity referred
    to in that paragraph is provided to them, does not comply with
    reasonable instructions to ensure their safety or that of any other
    person or the security of the penitentiary; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the prescribed circumstances, which
    circumstances may include, among other things, natural disasters,
    fires, riots and work refusals under section 128 of the [Canada
    Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), and those circumstances must be limited
    to what is reasonably required for security purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall maintain a record of every instance
  that an inmate has been offered an opportunity referred to in
  paragraph 36(1)(a) or (b) that the inmate refused, indicating the
  specific opportunity and any reason given for the refusal, or has not
  been given such an opportunity by reason of paragraph (1)(b) or (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 37
- 2012, c. 1, s. 61
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205876){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205876.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ongoing monitoring

- **[[37.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall ensure that
  measures are taken to provide for the ongoing monitoring of the health
  of inmates in a structured intervention unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mental health assessment and daily
  visits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall ensure that the measures include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a referral of the inmate's case, within 24 hours
    after the inmate's transfer into the structured intervention unit,
    to the portion of the Service that administers health care for the
    purpose of conducting a mental health assessment of the inmate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a visit to the inmate at least once every day by a
    registered health care professional employed or engaged by the
    Service.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205898){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205898.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mental health assessment

**[[37.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37.11 .sectionLabel}** If a staff member
or a person engaged by the Service believes that the confinement of an
inmate in a structured intervention unit is having detrimental impacts
on the inmate's health, the staff member or person shall refer, in the
prescribed manner, the inmate's case to the portion of the Service that
administers health care. Grounds for the belief include the inmate

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} refusing to interact with others;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} engaging in self-injurious behaviour;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} showing symptoms of a drug overdose; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} showing signs of emotional distress or exhibiting
  behaviour that suggests that they are in urgent need of mental health
  care.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205907){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205907.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations to institutional head

**[[37.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37.2 .sectionLabel}** A registered health
care professional employed or engaged by the Service may, for health
reasons, recommend to the institutional head that the conditions of
confinement of the inmate in a structured intervention unit be altered
or that the inmate not remain in the unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205913){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205913.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision --- institutional head

- **[[37.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall
  determine, in accordance with regulations made under paragraph 96(g),
  whether an inmate should remain in a structured intervention unit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as practicable after a registered health
    care professional recommends under section 37.2, for health reasons,
    that the inmate not remain in the unit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within the period that begins on the day on which
    the determination under subsection 29.01(2) is made and that ends on
    the expiry of the period of 30 days that begins on the first day on
    which the inmate is confined in the unit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} as soon as practicable in any of the prescribed
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of confinement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As soon as practicable after the registered health
  care professional referred to in section 37.2 has recommended, for
  health reasons, that the conditions of confinement be altered, the
  institutional head shall determine whether the inmate's conditions of
  confinement in the structured intervention unit should be altered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visit to the inmate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making a determination under this section, the
  institutional head shall visit the inmate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head shall maintain a record
  indicating the circumstances of every instance in which, because of
  security requirements, a visit was not face to face or took place
  through a cell door hatch.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No later than one working day after the day on which
  he or she makes a determination under this section, the institution
  head shall orally notify the inmate of the determination as well as
  the reasons for it and no later than two working days after the day on
  which the determination was made, the institutional head shall provide
  the inmate with those reasons in writing.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205930){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205930.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advice of registered health care
professional

- **[[37.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the institutional head determines
  under paragraph 37.3(1)(a) that an inmate should remain in a
  structured intervention unit or the institutional head determines
  under subsection 37.3(2) that an inmate's conditions of confinement in
  the structured intervention unit should not be altered in accordance
  with the recommendations of a registered health care professional,
  another registered health care professional shall provide advice to
  the committee established under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The registered health care professional providing the
  advice is to be a senior registered health care professional employed
  by the Service or a registered health care professional engaged by the
  Service as an expert advisor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall establish a committee
  consisting of staff members who hold a position higher in rank than
  that of institutional head for the purpose of making determinations
  under section 37.32.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 10](#1151516-1205941){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151516-1205941.html"
  role="button"}
:::
