[Previous
Version](section-46-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Searches of Inmates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106234 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Routine non-intrusive or frisk searches

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member may conduct routine non-intrusive searches or routine
  frisk searches of inmates, without individualized suspicion, in the
  prescribed circumstances, which circumstances must be limited to what
  is reasonably required for security purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person providing services of a prescribed class to
  the Service under a contract has the power to search that a staff
  member is authorized to conduct under subsection (1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conducting of such searches is provided for in
    the contract but does not constitute the person's principal services
    under the contract;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the searches are reasonably related to the person's
    principal services under the contract; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has received the prescribed training to
    conduct such searches.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 47
- 1995, c. 42, s. 14(F)
- [2019, c. 27, s. 13(F)](#1151525-1170963){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151525-1170963.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 47"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Routine strip search of inmates

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a staff
  member of the same sex as the inmate may conduct a routine strip
  search of an inmate, without individualized suspicion,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the prescribed circumstances in situations in
    which the inmate has been in a place where there was a likelihood of
    access to contraband that is capable of being hidden on or in the
    body; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the inmate is entering or leaving a structured
    intervention unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Choice of body scan search

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A body scan search of the inmate shall be conducted
  instead of the strip search if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the body scan search is authorized under section
    48.1; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed body scanner in proper working order
    is in the area where the strip search would be conducted.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 48
- [2019, c. 27, s. 14](#1151527-1206285){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151527-1206285.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-48-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 48"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search by body scan

**[[48.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48.1 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may,
in the prescribed circumstances, conduct a body scan search of an
inmate, and those circumstances must be limited to what is reasonably
required for security purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 15](#1151529-1488858){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151529-1488858.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frisk search of inmate

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a staff member suspects on reasonable grounds that an inmate is
  carrying contraband or carrying evidence relating to a disciplinary or
  criminal offence, the staff member may conduct a frisk search of the
  inmate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person providing services of a prescribed class to
  the Service under a contract has the powers of search of a staff
  member under subsection (1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conducting of such searches is provided for in
    the contract but does not constitute the person's principal services
    under the contract;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the searches are reasonably related to the person's
    principal services under the contract; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has received the prescribed training to
    conduct such searches.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strip search of inmate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a staff member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} believes on reasonable grounds that an inmate is
    carrying contraband or carrying evidence relating to a disciplinary
    or criminal offence, and that a strip search is necessary to find
    the contraband or evidence, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} satisfies the institutional head that there are
    reasonable grounds to so believe,

  a staff member of the same sex as the inmate may conduct a strip
  search of the inmate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency search

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a staff member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} satisfies the requirements of paragraph (3)(a), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} believes on reasonable grounds that the delay that
    would be necessary in order to comply with paragraph (3)(b) or with
    the gender requirement of subsection (3) would result in danger to
    human life or safety or in loss or destruction of the evidence,

  the staff member may conduct the strip search without complying with
  paragraph (3)(b) or the gender requirement of subsection (3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff member to inform institutional head

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** Where a staff member
believes on reasonable grounds that an inmate is carrying contraband in
a body cavity, the staff member may not seize or attempt to seize that
contraband, but shall inform the institutional head.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention in dry cell

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  institutional head is satisfied that there are reasonable grounds to
  believe that an inmate has ingested contraband or is carrying
  contraband in their rectum, the institutional head may authorize in
  writing the detention of the inmate in a cell without plumbing
  fixtures, on the expectation that the contraband will be expelled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visits by registered health care
  professional

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The inmate must be visited at least once every day by
  a registered health care professional.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 51
- [2019, c. 27, s. 16](#1151531-1488864){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151531-1488864.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 299](#1357481-1366417){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357481-1366417.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 301](#1357490-1488865){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357490-1488865.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-51-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 51"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Body cavity search

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** Where the institutional
head is satisfied that there are reasonable grounds to believe that an
inmate is carrying contraband in a body cavity and that a body cavity
search is necessary in order to find or seize the contraband, the
institutional head may authorize in writing a body cavity search to be
conducted by a qualified medical practitioner, if the inmate's consent
is obtained.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptional power of search

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the institutional head is satisfied that there are reasonable grounds
  to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there exists, because of contraband, a clear and
    substantial danger to human life or safety or to the security of the
    penitentiary, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a frisk search or strip search of all the inmates
    in the penitentiary or any part thereof is necessary in order to
    seize the contraband and avert the danger,

  the institutional head may authorize in writing such a search, subject
  to subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gender requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A strip search authorized under subsection (1) shall
  be conducted in each case by a staff member of the same sex as the
  inmate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Urinalysis

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 56 and
subsection 57(1), a staff member may demand that an inmate submit to
urinalysis

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the staff member believes on reasonable grounds
  that the inmate has committed or is committing the disciplinary
  offence referred to in paragraph 40(k) and that a urine sample is
  necessary to provide evidence of the offence, and the staff member
  obtains the prior authorization of the institutional head;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} as part of a prescribed random selection urinalysis
  program, conducted without individualized grounds on a periodic basis
  and in accordance with any Commissioner's Directives that the
  regulations may provide for; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where urinalysis is a prescribed requirement for
  participation in

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed program or activity involving contact
    with the community, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed substance abuse treatment program.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Urinalysis

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 56 and
subsection 57(2), a staff member, or any other person so authorized by
the Service, may demand that an offender submit to urinalysis

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at once, where the staff member or other authorized
  person has reasonable grounds to suspect that the offender has
  breached any condition of a temporary absence, work release, parole or
  statutory release that requires abstention from alcohol or drugs, in
  order to monitor the offender's compliance with that condition; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at regular intervals, in order to monitor the
  offender's compliance with any condition of a temporary absence, work
  release, parole or statutory release that requires abstention from
  alcohol or drugs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 55
- 1995, c. 42, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information requirements

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** Where a demand is made of
an offender to submit to urinalysis pursuant to section 54 or 55, the
person making the demand shall forthwith inform the offender of the
basis of the demand and the consequences of non-compliance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to make representations

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  inmate who is required to submit to urinalysis pursuant to paragraph
  54(a) shall be given an opportunity to make representations to the
  institutional head before submitting the urine sample.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An offender who is required to submit to urinalysis
  at regular intervals pursuant to section 55 shall be given reasonable
  opportunities to make representations to the prescribed official in
  relation to the length of the intervals.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring device

- **[[57.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Service may demand that an
  offender wear a monitoring device in order to monitor their compliance
  with a condition of a temporary absence, work release, parole,
  statutory release or long-term supervision that restricts their access
  to a person or a geographical area or requires them to be in a
  geographical area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An offender who is required to wear a monitoring
  device is to be given reasonable opportunities to make representations
  to the prescribed official in relation to the duration of the
  requirement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 64
:::

#### [Searches of Cells]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106310 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Searches of cells

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may, in
the prescribed manner, conduct searches of cells and their contents in
the prescribed circumstances, which circumstances must be limited to
what is reasonably required for security purposes.

#### [Searches of Visitors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106313 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[visitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[58.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58.1 .sectionLabel}** Despite the
definition [[visitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1), in
sections 59, 60 and 60.1 [[visitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} does not
include an [[immigration detainee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
section 94.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 386](#1468457-1479812){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468457-1479812.html"
  role="button"}
- [2024, c. 17, s. 392](#1468473-1488868){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468473-1488868.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-58.1-20240620.html "Link to previous version of section 58.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Routine non-intrusive or frisk searches

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may
conduct routine non-intrusive searches or routine frisk searches of
visitors, without individualized suspicion, in the prescribed
circumstances, which circumstances must be limited to what is reasonably
required for security purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 59
- [2019, c. 27, s. 17(F)](#1151533-1170968){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151533-1170968.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-59-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 59"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frisk search

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member may conduct a frisk search of a visitor where the staff
  member suspects on reasonable grounds that the visitor is carrying
  contraband or carrying other evidence relating to an offence under
  section 45.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strip search

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a staff member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} suspects on reasonable grounds that a visitor is
    carrying contraband or carrying other evidence relating to an
    offence under section 45 and believes that a strip search is
    necessary to find the contraband or evidence, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} satisfies the institutional head that there are
    reasonable grounds

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to suspect that the visitor is carrying
      contraband or carrying other evidence relating to an offence under
      section 45, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to believe that a strip search is necessary to
      find the contraband or evidence,

  a staff member of the same sex as the visitor may, after giving the
  visitor the option of voluntarily leaving the penitentiary forthwith,
  conduct a strip search of the visitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a staff member believes on reasonable grounds
  that a visitor is carrying contraband or carrying other evidence
  relating to an offence under section 45 and that a strip search is
  necessary to find the contraband or evidence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the staff member may detain the visitor in order to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} obtain the authorization of the institutional
      head to conduct a strip search, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} obtain the services of the police; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the staff member satisfies the institutional
    head that there are reasonable grounds to believe

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that the visitor is carrying contraband or
      carrying other evidence relating to an offence under section 45,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that a strip search is necessary to find the
      contraband or evidence,

    the institutional head may authorize a staff member of the same sex
    as the visitor to conduct a strip search of the visitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of detained visitor

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A visitor who is detained pursuant to subsection (3)
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be informed promptly of the reasons for the
    detention; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before being searched, be given a reasonable
    opportunity to retain and instruct counsel without delay and be
    informed of that right.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search by body scan

**[[60.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60.1 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may,
in the prescribed circumstances, conduct a body scan search of a
visitor, and those circumstances must be limited to what is reasonably
required for security purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 18](#1151535-1488872){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151535-1488872.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Searches of Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106339 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Routine searches

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member may, in the prescribed manner, conduct routine searches
  of vehicles at a penitentiary, without individualized suspicion, in
  the prescribed circumstances, which circumstances must be limited to
  what is reasonably required for security purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Searches for contraband

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A staff member who believes on reasonable grounds
  that contraband is located in a vehicle at a penitentiary in
  circumstances constituting an offence under section 45 may, with prior
  authorization from the institutional head, search the vehicle.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency searches

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a staff member believes on reasonable grounds
  that the delay that would be necessary in order to comply with the
  prior authorization requirement of subsection (2) would result in
  danger to human life or safety or the loss or destruction of the
  contraband, the staff member may search the vehicle without that prior
  authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptional power to search

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An institutional head may, in writing, authorize a
  staff member to search the vehicles at a penitentiary if the
  institutional head has reasonable grounds to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a clear and substantial danger to the
    security of the penitentiary or the life or safety of persons
    because evidence exists that there is contraband at the penitentiary
    or that a criminal offence is being planned or has been committed at
    the penitentiary; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is necessary to search the vehicles in order to
    locate and seize the contraband or other evidence and to avert the
    danger.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 61
- 2012, c. 1, s. 65
- [2019, c. 27, s. 19(F)](#1151537-1170969){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151537-1170969.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-61-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 61"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Warnings to be Posted]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106353 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warnings about searches

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** At each penitentiary, a
conspicuous warning shall be posted at the entrance to the lands and at
the visitor control point, stating that all visitors and vehicles at the
penitentiary are subject to being searched in accordance with this Part
and the regulations.

#### [Searches of Staff Members]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106356 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Routine non-intrusive or frisk searches

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may
conduct routine non-intrusive searches or routine frisk searches of
other staff members, without individualized suspicion, in the prescribed
circumstances, which circumstances must be limited to what is reasonably
required for security purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 63
- [2019, c. 27, s. 20(F)](#1151539-1170972){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151539-1170972.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frisk search or strip search

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a staff member believes on reasonable grounds that another staff
  member is carrying contraband or carrying evidence relating to a
  criminal offence and that a frisk search or strip search is necessary
  to find the contraband or evidence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the staff member may detain the other staff member
    in order to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} obtain the authorization of the institutional
      head to conduct a frisk search or strip search, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} obtain the services of the police; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the staff member satisfies the institutional
    head that there are reasonable grounds to believe that the other
    staff member is carrying contraband or carrying evidence relating to
    a criminal offence and that a frisk search or strip search is
    necessary to find the contraband or evidence, the institutional head
    may

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} authorize a staff member to conduct a frisk
      search of the other staff member, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} authorize a staff member of the same sex as the
      other staff member to conduct a strip search of that other staff
      member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of detained staff member

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A staff member who is detained pursuant to
  subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be informed promptly of the reasons for the
    detention; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before being searched, be given a reasonable
    opportunity to retain and instruct counsel without delay and be
    informed of that right.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search by body scan

**[[64.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64.1 .sectionLabel}** A staff member may,
in the prescribed circumstances, conduct a body scan search of another
staff member, and those circumstances must be limited to what is
reasonably required for security purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 21](#1151541-1488876){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151541-1488876.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Power to Seize]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106372 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to seize

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member may seize contraband, or evidence relating to a
  disciplinary or criminal offence, found in the course of a search
  conducted under sections 47 to 64, except a body cavity search or a
  body scan search.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A medical practitioner conducting a body cavity
  search may seize contraband or evidence relating to a disciplinary or
  criminal offence found in the course of that search.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person conducting a search pursuant to subsection
  47(2) or 49(2) may seize contraband found in the course of that
  search.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 65
- [2019, c. 27, s. 22](#1151543-1488878){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151543-1488878.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 300](#1357483-1366421){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357483-1366421.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Searches in Community-based Residential Facilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106380 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frisk search, room search

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee of a community-based residential facility who is so
  authorized by the Service may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct a frisk search of an offender in that
    facility, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} search an offender's room and its contents,

  where the employee suspects on reasonable grounds that the offender is
  violating or has violated a condition of the offender's parole,
  statutory release or temporary absence and that such a search is
  necessary to confirm the suspected violation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to seize

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee who conducts a search pursuant to
  subsection (1) may seize any evidence of a violation of the offender's
  conditions of release found in the course of the search.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[community-based
  residential facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[community-based residential
  facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a place that provides
  accommodation to offenders who are on parole, statutory release or
  temporary absence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 66
- 1995, c. 42, s. 71(F)
:::

#### [Reports Relating to Searches and Seizures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-106393 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports to be submitted

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** Reports in respect of
searches conducted pursuant to sections 47 to 66, and in respect of the
seizure of items in the course of those searches, must be submitted
where required by regulations made under paragraph 96(o) and in
accordance with those regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 67
- 1995, c. 42, s. 16
:::

### [General --- Living Conditions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106398 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instruments of restraint

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** No person shall apply an
instrument of restraint to an offender as punishment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cruel treatment, etc.

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
administer, instigate, consent to or acquiesce in any cruel, inhumane or
degrading treatment or punishment of an offender.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Living conditions, etc.

**[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall take
all reasonable steps to ensure that penitentiaries, the penitentiary
environment, the living and working conditions of inmates and the
working conditions of staff members are safe, healthful and free of
practices that undermine a person's sense of personal dignity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 70
- 1995, c. 42, s. 17(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contacts and visits

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  order to promote relationships between inmates and the community, an
  inmate is entitled to have reasonable contact, including visits and
  correspondence, with family, friends and other persons from outside
  the penitentiary, subject to such reasonable limits as are prescribed
  for protecting the security of the penitentiary or the safety of
  persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visitors' permitted items

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At each penitentiary, a conspicuous notice shall be
  posted at the visitor control point, listing the items that a visitor
  may have in possession beyond the visitor control point.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where visitor has non-permitted item

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a visitor has in possession, beyond the visitor
  control point, an item not listed on the notice mentioned in
  subsection (2) without having previously obtained the permission of a
  staff member, a staff member may terminate or restrict the visit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of Parliament, judges

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** Every member of the House
of Commons, every Senator and every judge of a court in Canada has the
right to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter any penitentiary,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} visit any part of a penitentiary, and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} visit any inmate, with the consent of the inmate,

subject to such reasonable limits as are prescribed for protecting the
security of the penitentiary or the safety of persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 72
- 1995, c. 42, s. 18(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assembly and association

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** Inmates are entitled to
reasonable opportunities to assemble peacefully and associate with other
inmates within the penitentiary, subject to such reasonable limits as
are prescribed for protecting the security of the penitentiary or the
safety of persons.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inmate input into decisions

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall provide
inmates with the opportunity to contribute to decisions of the Service
affecting the inmate population as a whole, or affecting a group within
the inmate population, except decisions relating to security matters.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Religion

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** An inmate is entitled to
reasonable opportunities to freely and openly participate in, and
express, religion or spirituality, subject to such reasonable limits as
are prescribed for protecting the security of the penitentiary or the
safety of persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 75
- 1995, c. 42, s. 19(F)
:::

### [Programs for Offenders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106430 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Programs for offenders generally

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall provide
a range of programs designed to address the needs of offenders and
contribute to their successful reintegration into the community.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Programs for female offenders

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** Without limiting the
generality of section 76, the Service shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide programs designed particularly to address the
  needs of female offenders; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} consult regularly about programs for female offenders
  with

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} appropriate women's groups, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} other appropriate persons and groups

  with expertise on, and experience in working with, female offenders.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payments to offenders

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} encouraging offenders to participate in programs
    provided by the Service, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing financial assistance to offenders to
    facilitate their reintegration into the community,

  the Commissioner may authorize payments to offenders at rates approved
  by the Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deductions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender receives a payment referred to in
  subsection (1) or income from a prescribed source, the Service may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make deductions from that payment or income in
    accordance with regulations made under paragraph 96(z.2) and any
    Commissioner's Directive; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require that the offender pay to Her Majesty in
    right of Canada, in accordance with regulations made pursuant to
    paragraph 96(*z.2.1*) and as set out in a Commissioner's Directive,
    an amount, not exceeding thirty per cent of the gross payment
    referred to in subsection (1) or gross income, for reimbursement of
    the costs of the offender's food and accommodation incurred while
    the offender was receiving that income or payment, or for
    reimbursement of the costs of work-related clothing provided to the
    offender by the Service.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 78
- 1995, c. 42, s. 20
:::

### [Indigenous Offenders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106452 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** In sections 79.1 to 84.1,

[[correctional services]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[correctional services]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means services or
    programs for offenders, including their care, custody and
    supervision. ([services correctionnels]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous governing body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous governing body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a council,
    government or other entity that is authorized to act on behalf of an
    Indigenous group, community or people that holds rights recognized
    and affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act,
    1982](/eng/Const/). ([corps dirigeant autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    organization with predominately Indigenous leadership. ([organisme
    autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned by the definition [[aboriginal peoples of
    Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 35(2) of the
    [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/).‍ ([peuples autochtones du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 79
- [2019, c. 27, s. 23](#1151545-1170982){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151545-1170982.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

- **[[79.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  making decisions under this Act affecting an Indigenous offender, the
  Service shall take the following into consideration:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} systemic and background factors affecting
    Indigenous peoples of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} systemic and background factors that have
    contributed to the overrepresentation of Indigenous persons in the
    criminal justice system and that may have contributed to the
    offender's involvement in the criminal justice system; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Indigenous culture and identity of the
    offender, including his or her family and adoption history.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- risk assessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The factors described in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) are
  not to be taken into consideration for decisions respecting the
  assessment of the risk posed by an Indigenous offender unless those
  factors could decrease the level of risk.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 23](#1151545-1170992){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151545-1170992.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Programs

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** Without limiting the
generality of section 76, the Service shall provide programs designed
particularly to address the needs of Indigenous offenders.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 80
- [2019, c. 27, s. 23](#1151545-1170997){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151545-1170997.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-80-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 80"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister, or a person authorized by the Minister, may enter into an
  agreement with an Indigenous governing body or any Indigenous
  organization for the provision of correctional services to Indigenous
  offenders and for payment by the Minister, or by a person authorized
  by the Minister, in respect of the provision of those services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), an agreement entered
  into under that subsection may provide for the provision of
  correctional services to a non-Indigenous offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Placement of offender

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In accordance with any agreement entered into under
  subsection (1), the Commissioner may transfer an offender to the care
  and custody of an appropriate Indigenous authority, with the consent
  of the offender and of the appropriate Indigenous authority.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 81
- 1995, c. 42, s. 21(F)
- [2019, c. 27, s. 24](#1151549-1171006){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151549-1171006.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advisory committees

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall establish a national Indigenous advisory committee, and
  may establish regional and local Indigenous advisory committees, which
  shall provide advice to the Service on the provision of correctional
  services to Indigenous offenders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committees to consult

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of carrying out their function under
  subsection (1), all committees shall consult regularly with Indigenous
  communities, Indigenous governing bodies, Indigenous organizations and
  other appropriate persons with knowledge of Indigenous matters.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 82
- [2019, c. 27, s. 25](#1151553-1171014){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151553-1171014.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-82-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 82"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Spiritual leaders and elders

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  greater certainty, Indigenous spirituality and Indigenous spiritual
  leaders and elders have the same status as other religions and other
  religious leaders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advice

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Service considers it appropriate in the
  circumstance, it shall seek advice from an Indigenous spiritual leader
  or elder when providing correctional services to an Indigenous inmate,
  particularly in matters of mental health and behaviour.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service shall take all reasonable steps to make
  available to Indigenous inmates the services of an Indigenous
  spiritual leader or elder after consultation with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the national Indigenous advisory committee
    established under section 82; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the appropriate regional and local Indigenous
    advisory committees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 83
- [2019, c. 27, s. 25](#1151553-1171026){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151553-1171026.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-83-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 83"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release into Indigenous community

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** If an inmate expresses an
interest in being released into an Indigenous community, the Service
shall, with the inmate's consent, give the community's Indigenous
governing body

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} adequate notice of the inmate's parole review or
  their statutory release date, as the case may be; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an opportunity to propose a plan for the inmate's
  release and integration into that community.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 84
- 2012, c. 1, s. 66
- [2019, c. 27, s. 25](#1151553-1171031){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151553-1171031.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-84-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 84"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans -- long-term supervision

**[[84.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84.1 .sectionLabel}** If an offender who is
required to be supervised by a long-term supervision order has expressed
an interest in being supervised in an Indigenous community, the Service
shall, with the offender's consent, give the community's Indigenous
governing body

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} adequate notice of the order; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an opportunity to propose a plan for the offender's
  release on supervision, and integration, into that community.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 17, s. 15
- [2019, c. 27, s. 25](#1151553-1171036){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151553-1171036.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-84.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 84.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Health Care]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106493 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** In sections 86 and 87,

[[health care]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[health care]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means medical care, dental care
    and mental health care, provided by registered health care
    professionals or by persons acting under the supervision of
    registered health care professionals; ([soins de
    santé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[mental health care]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[mental health care]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the care of a
    disorder of thought, mood, perception, orientation or memory that
    significantly impairs judgment, behaviour, the capacity to recognize
    reality or the ability to meet the ordinary demands of life; ([soins
    de santé mentale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[treatment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[treatment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means health care
    treatment. ([Version anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 85
- [2019, c. 27, s. 26](#1151557-1171040){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151557-1171040.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of Service

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Service shall provide every inmate with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} essential health care; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable access to non-essential health care.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The provision of health care under subsection (1)
  shall conform to professionally accepted standards.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 86
- [2019, c. 27, s. 27](#1151559-1171044){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151559-1171044.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 86"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Health care obligations

**[[86.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.1 .sectionLabel}** When health care is
provided to inmates, the Service shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} support the professional autonomy and the clinical
  independence of registered health care professionals and their freedom
  to exercise, without undue influence, their professional judgment in
  the care and treatment of inmates;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} support those registered health care professionals in
  their promotion, in accordance with their respective professional code
  of ethics, of patient-centred care and patient advocacy; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} promote decision-making that is based on the
  appropriate medical care, dental care and mental health care criteria.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 28](#1151561-1206118){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151561-1206118.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of health care unit

**[[86.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.2 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner may
designate a penitentiary or any area in a penitentiary to be a health
care unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 28](#1151561-1206125){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151561-1206125.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

**[[86.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.3 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of a
health care unit is to provide an appropriate living environment to
facilitate an inmate's access to health care.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 28](#1151561-1206129){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151561-1206129.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admission and discharge

**[[86.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.4 .sectionLabel}** The admission of
inmates to and the discharge of inmates from health care units must be
in accordance with regulations made under paragraph 96(g.2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 28](#1151561-1206133){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151561-1206133.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service to consider health factors

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall take
into consideration an offender's state of health and health care needs

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in all decisions affecting the offender, including
  decisions relating to placement, transfer, confinement in a structured
  intervention unit and disciplinary matters; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the preparation of the offender for release and
  the supervision of the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 87
- [2019, c. 27, s. 29](#1151563-1206144){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151563-1206144.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-87-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 87"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When treatment permitted

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as provided by subsection (5),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} treatment shall not be given to an inmate, or
    continued once started, unless the inmate voluntarily gives an
    informed consent thereto; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an inmate has the right to refuse treatment or
    withdraw from treatment at any time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of [[informed
  consent]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), an inmate's
  consent to treatment is informed consent only if the inmate has been
  advised of, and has the capacity to understand,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the likelihood and degree of improvement,
    remission, control or cure as a result of the treatment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any significant risk, and the degree thereof,
    associated with the treatment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any reasonable alternatives to the treatment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the likely effects of refusing the treatment; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the inmate's right to refuse the treatment or
    withdraw from the treatment at any time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special case

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), an inmate's
  consent to treatment shall not be considered involuntary merely
  because the treatment is a requirement for a temporary absence, work
  release or parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treatment demonstration programs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Treatment under a treatment demonstration program
  shall not be given to an inmate unless a committee that is independent
  of the Service and constituted as prescribed has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} approved the treatment demonstration program as
    clinically sound and in conformity with accepted ethical standards;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reviewed the inmate's consent to the treatment and
    determined that it was given in accordance with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where provincial law applies

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where an inmate does not have the capacity to
  understand all the matters described in paragraphs (2)(a) to (e), the
  giving of treatment to an inmate shall be governed by the applicable
  provincial law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Force-feeding

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall not
direct the force-feeding, by any method, of an inmate who had the
capacity to understand the consequences of fasting at the time the
inmate made the decision to fast.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Patient advocacy services

**[[89.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.1 .sectionLabel}** The Service shall
provide, in respect of inmates in penitentiaries designated by the
Commissioner, access to patient advocacy services

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to support inmates in relation to their health care
  matters; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to enable inmates and their families or an individual
  identified by the inmate as a support person to understand the rights
  and responsibilities of inmates related to health care.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 27, s. 30](#1151565-1206152){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151565-1206152.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Grievance or Complaint Procedure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106532 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grievance procedure

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** There shall be a
procedure for fairly and expeditiously resolving offenders' grievances
on matters within the jurisdiction of the Commissioner, and the
procedure shall operate in accordance with the regulations made under
paragraph 96(u).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to grievance procedure

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** Every offender shall have
complete access to the offender grievance procedure without negative
consequences.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 91
- 1995, c. 42, s. 22(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frivolous complaints, etc.

- **[[91.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Commissioner is satisfied that an offender has persistently
  submitted complaints or grievances that are frivolous, vexatious or
  not made in good faith, the Commissioner may, in accordance with the
  prescribed procedures, prohibit an offender from submitting any
  further complaint or grievance except by leave of the Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of prohibition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall review each prohibition under
  subsection (1) annually and shall give the offender written reasons
  for his or her decision to maintain or lift it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 3, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[91.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91.2 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations respecting the complaints and grievances
regime with respect to offenders who are subject to a prohibition under
subsection 91.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 3, s. 2
:::

### [Release of Inmates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106550 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** An inmate may be released
from a penitentiary or from any other place designated by the
Commissioner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Timing of release from penitentiary

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as provided by subsection (2), an inmate who is entitled to be
  released from penitentiary on a particular day by virtue of statutory
  release or the expiration of the sentence shall be released during
  normal business hours on the last working day before that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Earlier release in some cases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the institutional head is satisfied that an
  inmate's re-entry into the community will be facilitated by an earlier
  release than that provided for by subsection (1), the institutional
  head may release the inmate up to five days before the day on which
  the inmate is entitled to be released by virtue of statutory release
  or the expiration of the sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When inmate deemed released

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An inmate who is released pursuant to subsection (2)
  shall be deemed to have been released by virtue of statutory release
  or the expiration of the sentence, as the case may be, at the moment
  of actual release.

- [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2011, c. 11, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release on request

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an inmate who is in penitentiary pursuant to
  subsection 94(1) requests to be released, the Service shall release
  the inmate as soon as reasonably possible, but is not required to
  release the inmate except during normal business hours on a working
  day.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1995, c. 42, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 93
- 1995, c. 42, s. 23
- 2011, c. 11, s. 2
- 2012, c. 1, s. 67(F)
:::

Version](section-93-20110328.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Temporary Accommodation in Penitentiary]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106566 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary stay in penitentiary

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At the
  request of a person who has been or is entitled to be released from a
  penitentiary on parole or statutory release, the institutional head
  may allow them to stay temporarily in the penitentiary in order to
  assist their rehabilitation, but the temporary stay may not extend
  beyond the expiration of their sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person deemed an inmate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person staying temporarily in a penitentiary
  pursuant to subsection (1) shall be deemed to be an inmate while in
  the penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of parole or statutory
  release

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2), the parole or
  statutory release, as the case may be, of a person staying temporarily
  in a penitentiary pursuant to subsection (1) is deemed to be in force
  and subject to the provisions of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 94
- 1995, c. 42, s. 24
- 2012, c. 1, s. 68
:::

[Previous
Version](section-94-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Support to Canada Border Services Agency]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1479813 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[94.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.1 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in
this section apply in sections 94.2, 94.3 and 94.5 to 94.8.

[[designated immigrant station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated immigrant station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any area
    of a penitentiary designated under subsection 94.4(1). ([poste
    d'attente désigné]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[detention enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[detention enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person
    who is designated or authorized under section 6 of the [Immigration
    and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5). ([agent de
    détention]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[immigration detainee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[immigration detainee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
    detained under the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5). ([détenu de l'immigration]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479820){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479820.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Support

- **[[94.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Service may, in accordance with
  an arrangement entered into under section 94.3, provide support,
  including through the provision of services, to the Canada Border
  Services Agency

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to assist the Agency in the exercise of the powers
    and the performance of the duties and functions that relate to the
    [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) and are
    conferred on it under subsection 12(1) of the [Canada Border
    Services Agency Act](/eng/acts/C-1.4); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to assist detention enforcement officers in the
    exercise of the powers or the performance of the duties and
    functions that are conferred on them under the [Immigration and
    Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or that relate to that Act
    and are conferred on them under subsection 12(2) of the [Canada
    Border Services Agency Act](/eng/acts/C-1.4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The support referred to in subsection (1) must not
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the placement of a staff member in immediate charge
    or control of an [[immigrant station]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
    defined in subsection 142(2) of the [Immigration and Refugee
    Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 94.7(4), the search, escort,
    arrest or detention of an immigration detainee by a staff member; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 94.7(5), the provision of
    health care to an immigration detainee by a registered health care
    professional employed or engaged by the Service.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479832){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479832.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrangements

- **[[94.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may enter into an
  arrangement with the Canada Border Services Agency for the provision,
  by the Service, of support, including through the provision of
  services, to the Agency in order to assist it in the exercise of the
  powers or the performance of the duties and functions referred to in
  paragraph 94.2(1)(a) and to assist detention enforcement officers in
  the exercise of the powers or the performance of the duties and
  functions referred to in paragraph 94.2(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An arrangement is subject to the approval of the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory terms

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An arrangement must include terms that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specify the duration of the arrangement, the nature
    of the support to be provided and the name of a penitentiary of
    which an area may be the subject of a designation under subsection
    94.4(1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out a procedure --- to which every immigration
    detainee is to have complete access without negative consequences
    --- for fairly and expeditiously resolving immigration detainees'
    complaints with respect to activities carried out under an
    authorization granted under subsection 94.7(4) or health care
    provided under subsection 94.7(5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cost recovery

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An arrangement may provide for the recovery of costs
  incurred by the Service with respect to the provision of support to
  the Canada Border Services Agency.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479845){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479845.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[94.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  the written request of the President of the Canada Border Services
  Agency, the Commissioner may, for the purpose of implementing an
  arrangement entered into under subsection 94.3(1), designate any area
  of a penitentiary named in the arrangement for the purpose of
  subsection 142(3) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided in this Act or the
  regulations or in any other Act of Parliament or its regulations, an
  area designated under subsection (1) is deemed not to be a
  penitentiary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479852){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479852.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- access to penitentiary

- **[[94.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member or a detention enforcement officer must not permit an
  immigration detainee of a designated immigrant station adjacent to a
  penitentiary to have access to an area of the penitentiary unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the institutional head authorizes the access to the
    area so that the Service may provide support under subsection
    94.2(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the immigration detainee is escorted by a detention
    enforcement officer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} no inmate is present in the area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of paragraph (1)(c)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(c) does not apply with respect to an
  immigration detainee of a designated immigrant station adjacent to a
  penitentiary during any period that begins when the institutional head
  declares under paragraph 94.7(1)(a) that exigent circumstances exist
  with respect to the designated immigrant station and ends when the
  institutional head is satisfied that those exigent circumstances no
  longer exist.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479862){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479862.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- access to immigrant
station

- **[[94.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  staff member or detention enforcement officer must not permit an
  inmate of a penitentiary to have access to a designated immigrant
  station adjacent to the penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply with respect to an
  inmate of a penitentiary during any period that begins when the
  institutional head declares under paragraph 94.7(1)(b) that exigent
  circumstances exist with respect to the penitentiary and ends when the
  institutional head is satisfied that those exigent circumstances no
  longer exist if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the institutional head authorizes the access to the
    immigrant station; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the inmate is escorted by a staff member.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479871){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479871.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exigent circumstances --- declaration

- **[[94.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The institutional head of a
  penitentiary may declare in writing that exigent circumstances exist
  with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a designated immigrant station adjacent to the
    penitentiary, if the institutional head is satisfied that there are
    reasonable grounds to believe that there is a clear and substantial
    danger to the life or safety of persons in the designated immigrant
    station or to the security of the designated immigrant station; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the penitentiary, if the institutional head is
    satisfied that there are reasonable grounds to believe that there is
    a clear and substantial danger to the life or safety of persons in
    the penitentiary or to the security of the penitentiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- staff members and detention
  enforcement officers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the institutional head declares under
  subsection (1) that exigent circumstances exist with respect to a
  designated immigrant station adjacent to the penitentiary or with
  respect to the penitentiary adjacent to a designated immigrant
  station, the institutional head must immediately notify the staff
  members of the penitentiary and the detention enforcement officers of
  the designated immigrant station. When the institutional head is
  satisfied that the exigent circumstances no longer exist, the
  institutional head must immediately notify the staff members and
  detention enforcement officers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- portion of Service that
  administers health care

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the institutional head declares under paragraph
  (1)(a) that exigent circumstances exist with respect to a designated
  immigrant station, the institutional head must immediately notify the
  portion of the Service that administers health care. When the
  institutional head is satisfied that the exigent circumstances no
  longer exist, the institutional head must immediately notify that
  portion of the Service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional support

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the institutional head declares under paragraph
  (1)(a) that exigent circumstances exist with respect to a designated
  immigrant station, the institutional head may, during the period in
  which they are satisfied that the exigent circumstances exist,
  authorize a staff member to search, escort, arrest or detain an
  immigration detainee in the designated immigrant station to assist a
  detention enforcement officer in the exercise of the powers or the
  performance of the duties and functions referred to in paragraph
  94.2(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Health care

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} During the period that begins when the portion of the
  Service that administers health care is notified under subsection (3)
  that exigent circumstances exist with respect to a designated
  immigrant station and ends when it is notified under that subsection
  that the exigent circumstances no longer exist, a registered health
  care professional employed or engaged by the Service may provide
  health care to an immigration detainee of the designated immigrant
  station if the professional considers it necessary in order to
  preserve the life of, or treat a serious bodily injury suffered by,
  that detainee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of items

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If, in the course of activities carried out under an
  authorization granted under subsection (4), a staff member finds an
  item that the Canada Border Services Agency has prohibited within the
  designated immigrant station, the staff member must immediately
  deliver the item to a detention enforcement officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If an item is delivered to a detention enforcement
  officer under subsection (6), the detention enforcement officer may
  seize and hold the item under section 140 of the [Immigration and
  Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Peace officer

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In the course of activities carried out under an
  authorization granted under subsection (4), a staff member has all the
  powers, authority, protection and privileges that a peace officer has
  by law in respect of an immigration detainee.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479892){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479892.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of Parliament and judges

**[[94.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94.8 .sectionLabel}** Every member of the
House of Commons, every Senator and every judge of a court in Canada has
the right to enter any designated immigrant station, visit any part of a
designated immigrant station and visit any immigration detainee, with
the consent of the immigration detainee, subject to such reasonable
limits as are prescribed for protecting the security of the designated
immigrant station or the safety of persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 388](#1468460-1479896){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468460-1479896.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Annual Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106576 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall cause
to be laid before each House of Parliament, not later than the fifth
sitting day of that House after the 31st day of January next following
the end of each fiscal year, a report showing the operations of the
Service for that fiscal year.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106579 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the duties of staff members;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for authorizing staff members or classes of staff
  members to exercise powers, perform duties or carry out functions that
  this Part assigns to the Commissioner or the institutional head;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting, for the purposes of section 22,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which compensation may be
    paid,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} what constitutes a disability,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the manner of determining whether a person has a
    disability, and the extent of the disability,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} what constitutes an approved program,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} to whom compensation may be paid, and

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the compensation that may be paid, the time or
    times at which the compensation is to be paid, the terms and
    conditions in accordance with which the compensation is to be paid,
    and the manner of its payment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the placement of inmates pursuant to
  section 28 and their transfer pursuant to section 29;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} providing for the matters referred to in section 70;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting allowances, clothing and other necessities
  to be given to inmates when leaving penitentiary either temporarily or
  permanently;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the confinement of inmates in a structured
  intervention unit, including respecting the making of a determination
  by an institutional head, the Commissioner or the committee
  established under subsection 37.31(3) as to whether the conditions of
  confinement of an inmate in a structured intervention unit should be
  altered or as to whether an inmate should remain in such a unit;

- [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting the powers, duties and functions of
  independent external decision­makers, including respecting the making
  of a determination as to whether the conditions of confinement of an
  inmate in a structured intervention unit should be altered or as to
  whether an inmate should remain in such a unit;

- [(g.2)]{.lawlabel} respecting the admission of inmates to and the
  discharge of inmates from health care units;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the contents of the notice to be given to
  an inmate under section 42, and the time when the notice is to be
  given to the inmate;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} in connection with the disciplinary sanctions
  described in section 44,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the maximum of each of those sanctions,
    which maxima shall be higher for serious disciplinary offences than
    for minor ones,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} prescribing factors and guidelines to be
    considered or applied in imposing those sanctions,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the scope of each of those sanctions,
    and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} respecting the enforcement, suspension and
    cancellation of those sanctions;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} providing for a review of the decisions of the person
  or persons conducting a disciplinary hearing;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} providing for

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the appointment of persons other than staff members
    to conduct disciplinary hearings or to review decisions pursuant to
    regulations made under paragraph (j), and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the remuneration and travel and living expenses of
    persons referred to in subparagraph (i);

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner in which a search referred to
  in

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} paragraph (b) of the definition "frisk search" in
    section 46,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} paragraph (b) of the definition "non-intrusive
    search" in section 46, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} paragraph (b) of the definition "strip search" in
    section 46

  shall be carried out;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the procedures to be followed in
  conducting a urinalysis and the consequences of the results of a
  urinalysis;

- [(m.1)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the Commissioner to, by Commissioner's
  Directive, make rules regarding the consequences of tampering with or
  refusing to wear a monitoring device referred to in section 57.1;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the effect that a visitor's refusal to
  undergo a search can have on the visitor's right to visit an inmate or
  remain at the penitentiary;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} respecting

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the submission of reports referred to in section
    67, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the return or forfeiture of items seized under
    section 65 or subsection 66(2) or otherwise in possession of the
    Service;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the institutional head --- or a staff
  member designated by him or her --- to, in the prescribed
  circumstances, restrict or prohibit the entry into and removal from a
  penitentiary and the use by inmates of publications, video and audio
  materials, films and computer programs;

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} providing for inmates' moneys to be held in trust
  accounts;

- [(r)]{.lawlabel} respecting inmates' work and working conditions;

- [(s)]{.lawlabel} respecting penitentiary industry, including
  regulations authorizing the Minister to establish advisory boards and
  appoint members to them and regulations providing for the remuneration
  of those members at rates determined by the Treasury Board and for the
  reimbursement of any travel and living expenses that are consistent
  with directives of the Treasury Board and are incurred by those
  members in performing their duties while away from their ordinary
  place of residence;

- [(t)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conducting of businesses by inmates;

- [(u)]{.lawlabel} prescribing an offender grievance procedure;

- [(v)]{.lawlabel} for the organization, training, discipline,
  efficiency, administration and good management of the Service;

- [(w)]{.lawlabel} providing for inmates' access to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} legal counsel and legal reading materials,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} non-legal reading materials, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a commissioner for taking oaths and affidavits;

- [(x)]{.lawlabel} respecting inmates' attendance at judicial
  proceedings;

- [(y)]{.lawlabel} respecting the procedure to be followed on the death
  of an inmate, including the circumstances in which the Service may pay
  transportation, funeral, cremation or burial expenses for a deceased
  inmate;

- [(z)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the procedure governing the disposal of
  the effects of an escaped inmate;

- [(z.1)]{.lawlabel} for the delivery of the estate of a deceased inmate
  to the inmate's personal representative in accordance with the
  applicable provincial law;

- [(z.1.1)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the sources of income from which a
  deduction may be made pursuant to paragraph 78(2)(a) or in respect of
  which a payment may be required pursuant to paragraph 78(2)(b);

- [(z.2)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the purposes for which deductions may
  be made pursuant to paragraph 78(2)(a) and prescribing the amount or
  maximum amount of any deduction, which regulations may authorize the
  Commissioner to fix the amount or maximum amount of any deduction by
  Commissioner's Directive;

- [(z.2.1)]{.lawlabel} providing for the means of collecting the amount
  referred to in paragraph 78(2)(b), whether by transferring to Her
  Majesty moneys held in trust accounts established pursuant to
  paragraph 96(q) or otherwise, and authorizing the Commissioner to fix,
  by percentage or otherwise, that amount by Commissioner's Directive,
  and respecting the circumstances under which payment of that amount is
  not required;

- [(z.3)]{.lawlabel} providing for remuneration and travel and living
  expenses of members of committees established pursuant to subsection
  82(1);

- [(z.4)]{.lawlabel} for the involvement of members of the community in
  the operation of the Service;

- [(z.5)]{.lawlabel} prescribing procedures to be followed after the use
  of force by a staff member;

- [(z.6)]{.lawlabel} respecting the assignment to inmates of security
  classifications and subclassifications under section 30 and setting
  out the factors to be considered in determining the security
  classification and subclassification;

- [(z.7)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the institutional head --- or a staff
  member designated by him or her --- to, in the prescribed
  circumstances, monitor, intercept or prevent communications between an
  inmate and another person;

- [(z.8)]{.lawlabel} respecting escorted temporary absences ---
  including the circumstances in which the releasing authority may
  authorize an absence under section 17 --- and work releases;

- [(z.9)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner and form of making requests
  to the Commissioner under section 26 and respecting how those requests
  are to be dealt with;

- [(z.10)]{.lawlabel} imposing obligations or prohibitions on the
  Service for the purpose of giving effect to any provision of this
  Part;

- [(z.11)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that by this Part is to be
  prescribed; and

- [(z.12)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes and
  provisions of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 96
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 25, 72(F)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 69
- 2014, c. 36, s. 2(F)
- 2019, c. 27, s. 31
:::
