### [Rules]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106646 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this Part and
the regulations, the Commissioner may make rules

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the management of the Service;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the matters described in section 4; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes and
  provisions of this Part and the regulations.

### [Commissioner's Directives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106652 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner's Directives

- **[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commissioner may designate as Commissioner's Directives any or all
  rules made under section 97.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner's Directives shall be accessible to
  offenders, staff members and the public.

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Conditional Release, Detention and Long-term Supervision]{.HTitleText1} {#h-106658 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106659 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Parole Board of Canada
      continued by section 103 and includes a provincial parole board
      where it exercises jurisdiction in respect of parole as provided
      by section 112 or in respect of which any other provision of this
      Part is, by virtue of section 113, rendered
      applicable; ([Commission]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      Part I; ([commissaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[community-based residential facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[community-based residential facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has
      the same meaning as in subsection 66(3); ([établissement
      résidentiel communautaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[day parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[day parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the authority granted to
      an offender by the Board or a provincial parole board to be at
      large during the offender's sentence in order to prepare the
      offender for full parole or statutory release, the conditions of
      which require the offender to return to a penitentiary,
      community-based residential facility, provincial correctional
      facility or other location each night or at another specified
      interval; ([semi-liberté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[full parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[full parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the authority granted to
      an offender by the Board or a provincial parole board to be at
      large during the offender's sentence; ([libération conditionnelle
      totale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Indigenous]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Indigenous]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      I; ([autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[institutional head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[institutional head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in Part I; ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
      as in Part I; ([surveillance de longue durée]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      I; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person, other than a young person within the
        meaning of the [Youth Criminal Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5),
        who is under a sentence imposed before or after the coming into
        force of this section

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to an Act of Parliament or, to the
          extent that this Part applies, pursuant to a provincial Act,
          or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on conviction for criminal or civil contempt
          of court if the sentence does not include a requirement that
          the offender return to that court, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a young person within the meaning of the [Youth
        Criminal Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) with respect to whom an
        order, committal or direction under section 76, 89, 92 or 93 of
        that Act has been made,

      but does not include a person whose only sentence is a sentence
      being served intermittently pursuant to section 732 of the
      [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([délinquant]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means full parole or day
      parole; ([libération conditionnelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[parole supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parole supervisor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned
      by the definition [[staff member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
      subsection 2(1) or means a person entrusted by the Service with
      the guidance and supervision of an offender; ([surveillant de
      liberté conditionnelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      Part I; ([pénitencier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Ontario
      Board of Parole, la Commission québécoise des libérations
      conditionnelles or any other parole board established by the
      legislature or the lieutenant governor in council of a
      province; ([commission provinciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means regulations made by the
      Governor in Council pursuant to section
      156; ([règlement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou
      [réglementaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      I; ([peine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou [peine
      d'emprisonnement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[serious harm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[serious harm]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means severe physical injury
      or severe psychological damage; ([dommage grave]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      I; ([Service]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means release from
      imprisonment subject to supervision before the expiration of an
      offender's sentence, to which an offender is entitled under
      section 127; ([libération d'office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[statutory release date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[statutory release date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the date
      determined in accordance with section 127; ([date de libération
      d'office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[unescorted temporary absence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unescorted temporary absence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
      unescorted temporary absence from penitentiary authorized under
      section 116; ([permission de sortir sans escorte]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
      I; ([victime]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      Part I. ([jour ouvrable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to expiration of sentence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, a reference to the
  expiration according to law of the sentence of an offender shall be
  read as a reference to the day on which the sentence expires, without
  taking into account

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any period during which the offender could be
    entitled to statutory release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a youth sentence imposed under the
    [Youth Criminal Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5), the portion to be
    served under supervision in the community subject to conditions
    under paragraph 42(2)(n) of that Act or under conditional
    supervision under paragraph 42(2)(o), (q) or (r) of that Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any remission that stands to the credit of the
    offender on November 1, 1992.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers, etc.

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by this Part or by the
  regulations,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} powers, duties and functions assigned to the
    Commissioner by or pursuant to this Part may only be exercised or
    performed by the Commissioner or, where the Commissioner is absent
    or incapacitated or where the office is vacant, by the person acting
    in the place of the Commissioner; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} powers, duties and functions assigned to the
    institutional head by or pursuant to this Part may only be exercised
    or performed by the institutional head or, where the institutional
    head is absent or incapacitated or where the office is vacant, by
    the person who, at the relevant time, is in charge of the
    penitentiary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 99
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, ss. 26, 69(E), 70(E), 71(F)
- 1997, c. 17, s. 17
- 2002, c. 1, s. 173
- 2003, c. 22, s. 155
- 2012, c. 1, ss. 70, 160, 197
- [2019, c. 27, s. 32](#1151578-1171048){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151578-1171048.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20130228.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to persons subject to
long-term supervision order

**[[99.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99.1 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
required to be supervised by a long-term supervision order is deemed to
be an offender for the purposes of this Part, and sections 100, 101, 109
to 111 and 140 to 145 apply, with such modifications as the
circumstances require, to the person and to the long-term supervision of
that person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 17, s. 18
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Young persons

**[[99.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99.2 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, a young
person within the meaning of the [Youth Criminal Justice
Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) with respect to whom a committal or direction
under section 89, 92 or 93 of that Act has been made begins to serve his
or her sentence on the day on which the sentence comes into force in
accordance with subsection 42(12) of that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 1, s. 174
:::

### [Purpose and Principles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106709 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of conditional release

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of
conditional release is to contribute to the maintenance of a just,
peaceful and safe society by means of decisions on the timing and
conditions of release that will best facilitate the rehabilitation of
offenders and their reintegration into the community as law-abiding
citizens.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paramount consideration

**[[100.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100.1 .sectionLabel}** The protection of
society is the paramount consideration for the Board and the provincial
parole boards in the determination of all cases.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 71
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Principles guiding parole boards

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** The principles that
guide the Board and the provincial parole boards in achieving the
purpose of conditional release are as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} parole boards take into consideration all relevant
  available information, including the stated reasons and
  recommendations of the sentencing judge, the nature and gravity of the
  offence, the degree of responsibility of the offender, information
  from the trial or sentencing process and information obtained from
  victims, offenders and other components of the criminal justice
  system, including assessments provided by correctional authorities;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} parole boards enhance their effectiveness and
  openness through the timely exchange of relevant information with
  victims, offenders and other components of the criminal justice system
  and through communication about their policies and programs to
  victims, offenders and the general public;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} parole boards make the least restrictive
  determinations that are consistent with the protection of society;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} parole boards adopt and are guided by appropriate
  policies and their members are provided with the training necessary to
  implement those policies; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} offenders are provided with relevant information,
  reasons for decisions and access to the review of decisions in order
  to ensure a fair and understandable conditional release process.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 101
- 2012, c. 1, s. 71
- [2019, c. 27, s. 32.1](#1151583-1171050){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151583-1171050.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria for granting parole

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** The Board or a
provincial parole board may grant parole to an offender if, in its
opinion,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender will not, by reoffending, present an
  undue risk to society before the expiration according to law of the
  sentence the offender is serving; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the release of the offender will contribute to the
  protection of society by facilitating the reintegration of the
  offender into society as a law-abiding citizen.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 102
- 1995, c. 42, s. 27(F)
:::

### [Constitution and Jurisdiction of Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106731 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board continued

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** The National Parole
Board is continued as the Parole Board of Canada and consists of not
more than 60 full-time members and a number of part-time members all of
whom are appointed by the Governor in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister, to hold office during good behaviour for periods not
exceeding 10 years and three years, respectively.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 103
- 1993, c. 34, s. 57(F)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 73
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 103"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson and Executive
Vice-Chairperson

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
shall designate one of the full-time members of the Board to be its
Chairperson and, on the recommendation of the Minister, one of the
full-time members to be its Executive Vice-Chairperson.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Members appointed to the Board shall
  be sufficiently diverse in their backgrounds to be able to
  collectively represent community values and views in the work of the
  Board and to inform the community with respect to unescorted temporary
  absence, parole and statutory release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part-time members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A part-time member of the Board has the same powers
  and duties as a full-time member of the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Divisions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each member of the Board other than the Chairperson
  and the Executive Vice-Chairperson shall be assigned to a division of
  the Board specified in the instrument of appointment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All members of the Board are [ex officio]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} members of every division of the Board and may, with the
  approval of the Chairperson, sit on a panel of any division of the
  Board, subject to such conditions and during such periods as are
  approved by the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Policies

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Members of the Board shall exercise their functions
  in accordance with policies adopted pursuant to subsection 151(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 152(3), the review under this
  Part of any case within a particular class of cases shall be made by a
  panel that consists of at least the number of members of the Board
  specified in the regulations as the minimum number of members for
  cases of that class.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 105
- 1995, c. 42, s. 71(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitute members

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the event that a full-time member of the Board is absent or unable to
  act, the Governor in Council, on the recommendation of the Minister,
  may appoint a substitute member to act in the place of that member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A substitute member appointed pursuant to
  subsection (1) has all the powers and duties of a full-time member of
  the Board, subject to any limitation on those powers and duties that
  the Chairperson directs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of Board

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, the [Prisons
  and Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20), the [International Transfer of
  Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6), the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5), the [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes
  Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9) and the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the
  Board has exclusive jurisdiction and absolute discretion

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to grant parole to an offender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to terminate or to revoke the parole or statutory
    release of an offender, whether or not the offender is in custody
    under a warrant of apprehension issued as a result of the suspension
    of the parole or statutory release;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to cancel a decision to grant parole to an
    offender, or to cancel the suspension, termination or revocation of
    the parole or statutory release of an offender;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to review and to decide the case of an offender
    referred to it pursuant to section 129; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to authorize or to cancel a decision to authorize
    the unescorted temporary absence of an offender who is serving, in a
    penitentiary,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a life sentence imposed as a minimum punishment
      or commuted from a sentence of death,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sentence for an indeterminate period, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a sentence for an offence set out in Schedule I
      or II.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences under provincial Acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The jurisdiction of the Board under subsection (1)
  extends to any offender sentenced to a sentence imposed under a
  provincial Act that is to be served in a penitentiary pursuant to
  section 743.1 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), whether that
  sentence is to be served alone or concurrently with or consecutively
  to one or more other sentences imposed under an Act of Parliament or a
  provincial Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 107
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, ss. 28(E), 70(E), 71(F)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 110
- 2000, c. 24, s. 36
- 2004, c. 21, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-107-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 107"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction where no provincial board

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a provincial parole board has
  not been established in a province, the Board has, in respect of
  offenders serving sentences in a provincial correctional facility in
  that province, the same jurisdiction and discretion that it has in
  respect of offenders under paragraphs 107(1)(a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences under provincial Acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the jurisdiction of the
  Board under subsection (1) extends to any offender sentenced to a
  sentence imposed under a provincial Act that is to be served
  concurrently with or consecutively to a sentence imposed under an Act
  of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complementary legislation

  [[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_Ind11D2_hq_2231){#fn_Ind11D2_hq_2231-ID0EBCCA}[(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2)
  does not apply in a province until a day fixed by order of the
  Governor in Council made after the enactment of a provincial Act
  authorizing the Board to exercise the jurisdiction referred to in that
  subsection.

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_Ind11D2_hq_2231-ID0EBCCA){#fn_Ind11D2_hq_2231}Note:
    Subsection 108(2) applies in the Province of British Columbia as of
    April 19, 2007, *see* SI/2007-51.\]
    :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where subsection (3) does not apply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section shall be read without reference to
  subsection (3) with respect to any province in which subsection 14(1)
  of the Parole Act, as that Act read immediately before the coming into
  force of this section, was in force immediately before the coming into
  force of this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 108
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 69(E), 70(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation or variation of prohibition
orders

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** The Board may, on
application, cancel or vary the unexpired portion of a prohibition order
made under section 320.24 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)  or
section 259 of that Act, as it read immediately before the day on which
section 14 of An Act to amend the Criminal Code (offences relating to
conveyances) and to make consequential amendments to other Acts comes
into force, after a period of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ten years after the commencement of the order, in the
  case of a prohibition for life; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} five years after the commencement of the order, in
  the case of a prohibition for more than five years but less than life.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 109
- 2006, c. 14, s. 8
- 2018, c. 21, s. 47
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-109-20061214.html "Link to previous version of section 109"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clemency

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** The Board shall, when
so directed by the Minister, make or cause to be made any investigation
or inquiry desired by the Minister in connection with any request made
to the Minister for the exercise of the royal prerogative of mercy.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissemination of information

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** The Board shall
maintain

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a program of exchange of information with the other
  components of the criminal justice system; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a program to communicate its policies and programs to
  offenders, to victims of crime, to victims' groups, to other groups
  and organizations with a special interest in matters dealt with under
  this Part, and to the general public.

### [Constitution and Jurisdiction of Provincial Boards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106796 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of boards

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  provincial parole board for a province shall exercise jurisdiction in
  accordance with this Part in respect of the parole of offenders
  serving sentences in provincial correctional facilities in that
  province, other than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} offenders sentenced to life imprisonment as a
    minimum punishment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} offenders whose sentence has been commuted to life
    imprisonment; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} offenders sentenced to detention for an
    indeterminate period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Day parole jurisdiction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A provincial parole board may, but is not required
  to, exercise its jurisdiction under this section in relation to day
  parole.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 112
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 29(F), 69(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a provincial parole board has
  been established for a province, the lieutenant governor in council of
  the province may, by order, declare that all or any of the provisions
  of this Part that do not otherwise apply in respect of provincial
  parole boards shall apply in respect of that provincial parole board
  and offenders under its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The lieutenant governor in council of a province may,
  in respect of the provincial parole board for the province and
  offenders under its jurisdiction, make regulations in the same manner
  and for the same purposes as the Governor in Council may make
  regulations pursuant to section 156 in respect of the Board and
  offenders under its jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 113
- 1995, c. 42, s. 30(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of province of residence

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any agreement entered
  into pursuant to this section, an offender who is released on parole
  in one province and moves to another province remains under the
  jurisdiction of the board that granted the parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal-provincial agreements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister, with the approval of the Governor in
  Council, may enter into an agreement with the government of a province
  for which a provincial parole board has been established for the
  transfer of jurisdiction in respect of offenders who move to the
  province after their release by the Board on parole from a provincial
  correctional facility in another province for which no provincial
  parole board has been established.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The government of a province for which a provincial
  parole board has been established may enter into an agreement with the
  Government of Canada for the transfer to the Board of jurisdiction in
  respect of offenders released on parole by the provincial parole board
  who move to a province for which no provincial parole board has been
  established.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interprovincial agreements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The governments of provinces may enter into
  agreements with one another for the transfer of jurisdiction in
  respect of offenders released on parole by one provincial parole board
  who move to the territorial jurisdiction of another provincial parole
  board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statutory release

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (4) apply, with such modifications
  as the circumstances require, in respect of offenders released on
  statutory release.

### [Unescorted Temporary Absence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106825 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum time to be served

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  portion of a sentence that must be served before an offender serving a
  sentence in a penitentiary may be released on an unescorted temporary
  absence is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender serving a life sentence,
    other than an offender referred to in paragraph (a.1), the period
    required to be served by the offender to reach the offender's full
    parole eligibility date less three years;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender described in
    subsection 746.1(3) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the
    longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period that expires when all but one fifth of
      the period of imprisonment the offender is to serve without
      eligibility for parole has been served, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender serving a sentence for
    an indeterminate period, other than an offender referred to in
    paragraph (b.1), the longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with section 761 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), less three years, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender serving a sentence for
    an indeterminate period as of the date on which this paragraph comes
    into force, the longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} three years, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} six months, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one half of the period required to be served by
      the offender to reach their full parole eligibility date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an offender whose
  life or health is in danger and for whom an unescorted temporary
  absence is required in order to administer emergency medical
  treatment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum security

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Offenders who, pursuant to subsection 30(1) and the
  regulations made under paragraph 96(z.6), are classified as maximum
  security offenders are not eligible for an unescorted temporary
  absence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 115
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 31, 71(F)
- 1997, c. 17, s. 19
- 2012, c. 1, s. 74
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-115-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 115"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for authorization

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may authorize the unescorted temporary absence of an offender
  referred to in paragraph 107(1)(e) where, in the opinion of the Board,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender will not, by reoffending, present an
    undue risk to society during the absence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is desirable for the offender to be absent from
    the penitentiary for medical, administrative, community service,
    family contact, including parental responsibilities, personal
    development for rehabilitative purposes or compassionate reasons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the offender's behaviour while under sentence does
    not preclude authorizing the absence; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a structured plan for the absence has been
    prepared.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner or the institutional head may
  authorize the unescorted temporary absence of an offender, other than
  an offender referred to in paragraph 107(1)(e), where, in the opinion
  of the Commissioner or the institutional head, as the case may be, the
  criteria set out in paragraphs (1)(a) to (d) are met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical reasons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An unescorted temporary absence for medical reasons
  may be authorized for an unlimited period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal development or community
  service

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), an unescorted temporary
  absence for reasons of community service or personal development may
  be authorized for a maximum of fifteen days, at the rate of not more
  than three times a year for an offender classified by the Service as a
  medium security offender and not more than four times a year for an
  offender classified as a minimum security offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervals

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An unescorted temporary absence authorized for
  reasons referred to in subsection (4) must be followed by a period of
  custody of at least seven days before the next such absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An unescorted temporary absence for purposes of a
  specific personal development program may be authorized for a maximum
  of sixty days and may be renewed, for periods of up to sixty days
  each, for the purposes of the program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absences for other reasons

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Unescorted temporary absences for reasons other than
  those referred to in subsection (3) or (4) may be authorized for a
  maximum total of forty-eight hours per month for an offender
  classified by the Service as a medium security offender, and for a
  maximum total of seventy-two hours per month for an offender
  classified as a minimum security offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The circumstances and manner in which, and the time
  at which, an application for an unescorted temporary absence must be
  made shall be prescribed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel time

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the period authorized for the purposes
  of an unescorted temporary absence, an offender may be granted the
  time necessary to travel to and from the place where the absence is
  authorized to be spent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of absence

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Board, the Commissioner or the institutional
  head, whichever authorized a particular unescorted temporary absence
  of an offender, may cancel that absence, either before or after its
  commencement,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the cancellation is considered necessary and
    reasonable to prevent a breach of a condition of the absence or
    where such a breach has occurred;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the grounds for granting the absence have
    changed or no longer exist; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after a review of the offender's case based on
    information that could not reasonably have been provided when the
    absence was authorized.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 116
- 1993, c. 34, s. 58(F)
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 32(F), 71(F)
- [2019, c. 27, s. 33(E)](#1151585-1171052){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151585-1171052.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-116-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 116"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation to Commissioner

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may confer on the Commissioner or the institutional head, for
  such period and subject to such conditions as it specifies, any of its
  powers under section 116 in respect of any class of offenders or class
  of absences.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation to provincial hospital

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to an agreement under paragraph
  16(1)(a), an offender referred to in paragraph 107(1)(e) or subsection
  116(2) has been admitted to a hospital operated by a provincial
  government in which the liberty of persons is normally subject to
  restrictions, the Board, the Commissioner or the institutional head,
  as the case may be, may confer on the person in charge of the
  hospital, for such period and subject to such conditions as they
  specify, any of their respective powers under section 116 in relation
  to that offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension by institutional head

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board has not authorized the Commissioner
  or the institutional head under subsection (1) in respect of the
  offender or in respect of the absence, the institutional head of the
  penitentiary from which an unescorted temporary absence has been
  effected may suspend the absence if, in the opinion of the
  institutional head, the offender's retention in custody or
  recommitment to custody is justified in order to protect society, on
  the basis of information that could not reasonably have been provided
  to the Board when the absence was authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral of suspension to Board

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An institutional head who suspends the unescorted
  temporary absence of an offender shall forthwith refer the offender's
  case to the Board, and the Board shall decide whether the absence
  should be cancelled.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 117
- 1995, c. 42, s. 71(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant for arrest and recommittal

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** A person who cancels an
unescorted temporary absence pursuant to subsection 116(10) or pursuant
to a delegation of power under subsection 117(1) or (2), or who suspends
an unescorted temporary absence pursuant to subsection 117(3), shall
cause a warrant in writing to be issued authorizing the apprehension and
recommitment to custody of the offender pursuant to section 137, where
the offender is not in custody in a penitentiary or in a hospital
referred to in subsection 117(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 118
- 1995, c. 42, s. 71(F)
:::

### [Eligibility for Parole]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106893 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time when eligible for day parole

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 746.1 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the [National
  Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes
  Against Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), the portion of
  a sentence that must be served before an offender may be released on
  day parole is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one year, where the offender was, before October
    15, 1977, sentenced to preventive detention;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the offender is an offender, other than an
    offender referred to in paragraph (b.1), who was sentenced to
    detention in a penitentiary for an indeterminate period, the longer
    of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with section 761 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), less three years, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} where the offender was sentenced to detention in
    a penitentiary for an indeterminate period as of the date on which
    this paragraph comes into force, the longer of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} three years, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period required to be served by the offender
      to reach the offender's full parole eligibility date, determined
      in accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the offender is serving a sentence of two
    years or more, other than a sentence referred to in paragraph (a) or
    (b), the greater of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the portion ending six months before the date on
      which full parole may be granted, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} six months; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} one half of the portion of the sentence that must
    be served before full parole may be granted, where the offender is
    serving a sentence of less than two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time when eligible for day parole

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 746.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against
  Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), an offender described
  in subsection 746.1(1) or (2) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  or to whom those subsections apply pursuant to subsection 226.1(2) of
  the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) or subsection 15(2) of the
  [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), shall
  not, in the circumstances described in subsection 120.2(2) or (3), be
  released on day parole until three years before the day that is
  determined in accordance with subsection 120.2(2) or (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When eligible for day parole --- young
  offender sentenced to life imprisonment

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 746.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against
  Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), in the circumstances
  described in subsection 120.2(2), the portion of the sentence of an
  offender described in subsection 746.1(3) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or to whom that subsection applies pursuant to
  subsection 226.1(2) of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) or
  subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes
  Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9) that must be served before the offender may be
  released on day parole is the longer of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period that expires when all but one fifth of
    the period of imprisonment the offender is to serve without
    eligibility for parole has been served, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the portion of the sentence that must be served
    before full parole may be granted to the offender, determined in
    accordance with subsection 120.2(2), less three years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short sentences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board is not required to review the case of an
  offender who applies for day parole if the offender is serving a
  sentence of less than six months.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 119
- 1995, c. 22, ss. 13, 18, c. 42, ss. 33, 69(E)
- 1997, c. 17, s. 20
- 1998, c. 35, s. 111
- 2000, c. 24, s. 37
- 2013, c. 24, s. 127
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[119.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 119.2 to 120.3, and unless the context requires otherwise,
[[sentence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a sentence that is not
constituted under subsection 139(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 17, s. 21
- 2011, c. 11, s. 3
- 2012, c. 1, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119.1-20110328.html "Link to previous version of section 119.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Youth sentence

**[[119.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.2 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 120 to 120.3, the eligibility for parole of a young person in
respect of whom a youth sentence is imposed under paragraph 42(2)(n),
(o), (q) or (r) of the [Youth Criminal Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) and
who is transferred to a provincial correctional facility for adults or a
penitentiary under section 89, 92 or 93 of that Act shall be determined
on the basis of the total of the custody and supervision periods of the
youth sentence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time when eligible for full parole

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 746.1 and 761 of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) and to any order made under
  section 743.6 of that Act, to subsection 226.1(2) of the [National
  Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and to any order made under section 226.2
  of that Act, and to subsection 15(2) of the [Crimes Against Humanity
  and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), an offender is not eligible for
  full parole until the day on which the offender has served a period of
  ineligibility of the lesser of one third of the sentence and seven
  years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Life sentence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any order made under section 743.6 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 226.2 of the [National
  Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), an offender who is serving a life
  sentence, imposed otherwise than as a minimum punishment, is not
  eligible for full parole until the day on which the offender has
  served a period of ineligibility of seven years less any time spent in
  custody between the day on which the offender was arrested and taken
  into custody, in respect of the offence for which the sentence was
  imposed, and the day on which the sentence was imposed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 120
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, s. 34
- 1998, c. 35, s. 112
- 2000, c. 24, s. 38
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 126, 128
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple sentences on same day

- **[[120.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is not serving a
  sentence and who receives more than one sentence on the same day is
  not eligible for full parole until the day on which they have served a
  period equal to the total of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of any
    portion of the sentence constituted under subsection 139(1) that is
    subject to an order under section 743.6 of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 226.2 of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of any other
    portion of that sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One or more additional consecutive
  sentences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an offender who is serving a sentence, or is
  serving a sentence that was constituted under subsection 139(1),
  receives an additional sentence that is to be served consecutively to
  the sentence they are serving when the additional sentence is imposed
  --- or receives, on the same day, two or more additional sentences to
  be served consecutively and the additional sentences are to be served
  consecutively to the sentence they are serving when the additional
  sentences are imposed --- the offender is not eligible for full parole
  until the day on which they have served, from the day on which the
  additional sentence is or sentences are imposed, the total of the
  following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remaining period of ineligibility in respect of
    the sentence they are serving when the additional sentence is or
    sentences are imposed, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of the
    additional sentence or, in the case of two or more additional
    sentences, a period equal to the total of the periods of
    ineligibility in respect of all of the additional sentences.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional sentence to be served
  consecutively to portion of sentence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), if an offender who is serving
  a sentence or a sentence that was constituted under subsection 139(1)
  receives an additional sentence or two or more sentences that are to
  be served consecutively to a portion of the sentence they are serving
  when the additional sentence is imposed --- or receives, on the same
  day, two or more additional sentences including a sentence to be
  served concurrently with the sentence being served and one or more
  sentences to be served consecutively to the additional concurrent
  sentence --- they are not eligible for full parole until the day on
  which they have served, from the day on which the additional sentence
  is or sentences are imposed, any remaining period of ineligibility to
  which they are subject and the longer of the following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one third of the period that equals the difference
    between the length of the sentence that was constituted under
    subsection 139(1), including the additional sentence or sentences,
    and the length of the sentence that they are serving when the
    additional sentence is or sentences are imposed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility of the additional
    sentence that is or sentences that are ordered to be served
    consecutively.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 42, s. 34
- 1997, c. 17, s. 22(F)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 76
- 2013, c. 24, s. 133
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120.1-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 120.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional concurrent sentence

- **[[120.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if an
  offender who is serving a sentence, or is serving a sentence that was
  constituted under subsection 139(1), receives an additional sentence
  that is to be served concurrently with the sentence they are serving
  when the additional sentence is imposed, they are not eligible for
  full parole until the day that is the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which they have served the period of
    ineligibility in respect of the sentence they are serving when the
    additional sentence is imposed, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which they have served

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of any
      portion, of the sentence that includes the additional sentence as
      provided by subsection 139(1), that is subject to an order under
      section 743.6 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section
      226.2 of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of any
      other portion of that sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One or more sentences in addition to
  life sentence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an offender who is serving a life sentence or a
  sentence for an indeterminate period receives a sentence for a
  determinate period --- or receives, on the same day, two or more
  sentences for a determinate period --- they are not eligible for full
  parole until the day on which they have served, from the day on which
  the additional sentence is or sentences are imposed, the total of the
  following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remaining period of ineligibility to which they
    are subject, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of the
    additional sentence or, in the case of two or more additional
    sentences, the period of ineligibility --- determined in accordance
    with subsection (1) or section 120.1, as the case may be --- in
    respect of the additional sentences.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of period of ineligibility
  for parole

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If there has been a reduction --- under section 745.6
  of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the
  [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) or subsection 15(2) of the
  [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9) --- in
  the number of years of imprisonment without eligibility for parole of
  an offender referred to in subsection (2), the offender is not
  eligible for full parole until the day on which they have served, from
  the day on which the additional sentence is or sentences are imposed,
  the total of the following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remaining period of ineligibility to which they
    would have been subject after taking into account the reduction, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of ineligibility in respect of the
    additional sentence or, in the case of two or more additional
    sentences, the period of ineligibility --- determined in accordance
    with subsection (1) or section 120.1, as the case may be --- in
    respect of the additional sentences.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 22, s. 18, c. 42, s. 34
- 1997, c. 17, s. 23(F)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 113
- 2000, c. 24, s. 39
- 2012, c. 1, s. 76
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 127, 128
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120.2-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 120.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period

**[[120.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120.3 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section
745 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(1) of the
[National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(1) of the
[Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9), the day
on which an offender is eligible for full parole shall not be later than

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who is not serving a sentence
  and receives more than one sentence on the same day, the day on which
  they have served 15 years from the day on which the sentences are
  imposed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender who is serving a sentence
  --- or is serving a sentence that was constituted under subsection
  139(1) --- and who receives an additional sentence that changes the
  day on which they are eligible for parole, the day on which they have
  served 15 years from the day on which the additional sentence is
  imposed; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender who is serving a sentence
  --- or is serving a sentence that was constituted under subsection
  139(1) --- and who receives, on the same day, two or more additional
  sentences that change the day on which they are eligible for parole,
  the day on which they have served 15 years from the day on which the
  additional sentences are imposed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 22, s. 18, c. 42, s. 34
- 1998, c. 35, s. 114
- 2000, c. 24, s. 40
- 2012, c. 1, s. 76
- 2013, c. 24, s. 127
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120.3-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 120.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptional cases

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 102 --- and
  despite sections 119 to 120.3 of this Act, sections 746.1 and 761 of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), subsection 226.1(2) of the
  [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) and subsection 15(2) of the
  [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9) and any
  order made under section 743.6 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  or section 226.2 of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) ---
  parole may be granted at any time to an offender

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} who is terminally ill;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whose physical or mental health is likely to suffer
    serious damage if the offender continues to be held in confinement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for whom continued confinement would constitute an
    excessive hardship that was not reasonably foreseeable at the time
    the offender was sentenced; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} who is the subject of an order of surrender under
    the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01) and who is to be detained
    until surrendered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs (1)(b) to (d) do not apply to an offender
  who is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} serving a life sentence imposed as a minimum
    punishment or commuted from a sentence of death; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} serving, in a penitentiary, a sentence for an
    indeterminate period.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 121
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, s. 35
- 1998, c. 35, s. 115
- 1999, c. 18, s. 86
- 2012, c. 1, s. 77
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 128, 133
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Parole Reviews]{.HTitleText2} {#h-106985 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Day parole review

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 119(2), the
  Board shall, on application, at the time prescribed by the
  regulations, review, for the purpose of day parole, the case of every
  offender other than an offender referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special cases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board may, on application, at the time prescribed
  by the regulations, review, for the purpose of day parole, the case of
  an offender who is serving a sentence of two years or more in a
  provincial correctional facility in a province in which no program of
  day parole has been established for that category of offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision or adjournment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} With respect to a review commenced under this
  section, the Board shall decide whether to grant day parole, or may
  adjourn the review for a reason authorized by the regulations and for
  a reasonable period not exceeding the maximum period prescribed by the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application for one year

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No application for day parole may be made until one
  year after the date of the Board's decision --- or until any earlier
  time that the regulations prescribe or the Board determines --- if,
  following a review, the Board does not grant day parole or cancels or
  terminates parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum duration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Day parole may be granted to an offender for a period
  not exceeding six months, and may be continued for additional periods
  not exceeding six months each following reviews of the case by the
  Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of application

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An offender may not withdraw an application for day
  parole within 14 days before the commencement of the review unless the
  withdrawal is necessary and it was not possible to withdraw it earlier
  due to circumstances beyond their control.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 122
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 36(F), 69(E)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 78
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full parole review

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board shall, within the period prescribed by the regulations and for
  the purpose of deciding whether to grant full parole, review the case
  of every offender who is serving a sentence of two years or more and
  who is not within the jurisdiction of a provincial parole board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board is not required under subsection (1), (5)
  or (5.1) to review the case of an offender who has advised the Board
  in writing that they do not wish to be considered for full parole and
  who has not in writing revoked that advice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review by Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall, on application within the period
  prescribed by the regulations, review, for the purpose of full parole,
  the case of every offender who is serving a sentence of less than two
  years in a penitentiary or provincial correctional facility in a
  province where no provincial parole board has been established.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short sentences

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The Board is not required to review the case of an
  offender who applies for full parole if the offender is serving a
  sentence of less than six months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision or adjournment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} With respect to a review commenced under this
  section, the Board shall decide whether to grant full parole, or may
  grant day parole, or may adjourn the review for a reason authorized by
  the regulations and for a reasonable period not exceeding the maximum
  period prescribed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further review --- Board does not grant
  parole

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Board decides not to grant parole following a
  review under subsection (1) or section 122 or if a review is not made
  by virtue of subsection (2), the Board shall conduct another review
  within two years after the later of the day on which the review took
  place or was scheduled to take place and thereafter within two years
  after that day until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender is released on full parole or on
    statutory release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offender's sentence expires; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} less than four months remain to be served before
    the offender's statutory release date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violent offender

  [(5.01)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (5), if the Board decides not
  to grant parole to an offender who has been convicted of an offence
  involving violence for which the offender is serving a sentence of at
  least two years --- or a sentence that includes a sentence of at least
  two years for an offence involving violence --- following a review
  under subsection (1) or section 122, or if a review is not made by
  virtue of subsection (2), the Board shall conduct another review
  within five years after the later of the day on which the review took
  place or was scheduled to take place and thereafter within five years
  after that day until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender is released on full parole or on
    statutory release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offender's sentence expires; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} less than four months remain to be served before
    the offender's statutory release date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written reasons

  [(5.02)]{.lawlabel} If the Board decides not to grant parole to an
  offender referred to in subsection (5.01), it shall provide the
  offender with written reasons for its decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further review --- Board terminates or
  cancels parole

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Board cancels or terminates parole, it shall
  conduct another review within two years after the cancellation or
  termination and, after that date, within two years after the day on
  which each preceding review takes place until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender is released on full parole or
    statutory release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offender's sentence expires; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} less than four months remain to be served before
    the offender's statutory release date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violent offender

  [(5.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (5.1), if the Board cancels or
  terminates parole for an offender who has been convicted of an offence
  involving violence for which the offender is serving a sentence of at
  least two years --- or a sentence that includes a sentence of at least
  two years for an offence involving violence --- the Board shall
  conduct another review within four years after the date of
  cancellation or termination and, after that date, within five years
  after the day on which each preceding review takes place until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender is released on full parole or on
    statutory release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offender's sentence expires; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} less than four months remain to be served before
    the offender's statutory release date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application for one year

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No application for full parole may be made until one
  year after the date of the Board's decision --- or until any earlier
  time that the regulations prescribe or the Board determines --- if,
  following a review, the Board does not grant full parole or cancels or
  terminates parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An offender may not withdraw an application for full
  parole within 14 days before the commencement of the review unless the
  withdrawal is necessary and it was not possible to withdraw it earlier
  due to circumstances beyond their control.

- Definition of [[offence involving violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[offence involving
  violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means murder or any offence set out in
  Schedule I.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 123
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 37, 69(E)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 79
- 2015, c. 11, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Urine sample positive or not provided

**[[123.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.1 .sectionLabel}** If an offender has
been granted parole under section 122 or 123 but has not yet been
released and the offender fails or refuses to provide a urine sample
when demanded to provide one under section 54, or provides under that
section a urine sample for which the result of the urinalysis is
positive, as that term is defined in the regulations, then the Service
shall inform the Board of the failure or refusal or the test result.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 30, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offenders unlawfully at large

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board is not required to review the case of an offender who is
  unlawfully at large during the period prescribed by the regulations
  for a review under section 122 or 123 but it shall review the case as
  soon as possible after being informed of the offender's return to
  custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Timing of release

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender is granted parole but no date is
  fixed for the offender's release, the parole shall take effect, and
  the offender shall be released, forthwith after such period as is
  necessary to implement the decision to grant parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of parole

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an offender has been granted parole under section
  122 or 123, the Board may, after a review of the case based on
  information that could not reasonably have been provided to it at the
  time parole was granted, cancel the parole if the offender has not
  been released or terminate the parole if the offender has been
  released.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of parole --- drug test

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Board is informed of the matters under
  section 123.1 and the offender has still not yet been released, the
  Board shall can- cel the parole if, in its opinion, based on the
  information received under that section, the criteria set out in
  paragraphs 102(a) and (b) are no longer met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Board exercises its power under subsection
  (3), it shall, within the period prescribed by the regulations, review
  its decision and either confirm or cancel it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 124
- 1995, c. 42, s. 38
- 2011, c. 11, s. 4
- 2012, c. 1, s. 80, c. 19, s. 526
- 2015, c. 30, s. 3
:::

:::

### [Statutory Release]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107062 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any provision of this
  Act, an offender sentenced, committed or transferred to penitentiary
  is entitled to be released on the date determined in accordance with
  this section and to remain at large until the expiration of the
  sentence according to law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence for past offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, the statutory release date
  of an offender sentenced before November 1, 1992 to imprisonment for
  one or more offences shall be determined by crediting against the
  sentence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remission, statutory or earned, standing to the
    offender's credit on that day; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum remission that could have been earned
    on the balance of the sentence pursuant to the Penitentiary Act or
    the [Prisons and Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20), as those Acts
    read immediately before that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence for future offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, the statutory release date
  of an offender sentenced on or after November 1, 1992 to imprisonment
  for one or more offences is the day on which the offender completes
  two thirds of the sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentences for past and future offences

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, the statutory release date
  of an offender sentenced before November 1, 1992 to imprisonment for
  one or more offences and sentenced on or after November 1, 1992 to
  imprisonment for one or more offences is the later of the dates
  determined in accordance with subsections (2) and (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If parole or statutory release revoked

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections 130(4) and (6), the statutory
  release date of an offender whose parole or statutory release is
  revoked is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which they have served two thirds of the
    unexpired portion of the sentence after being recommitted to custody
    as a result of a suspension or revocation under section 135; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an additional sentence is imposed after the
    offender is recommitted to custody as a result of a suspension or
    revocation under section 135, the day on which they have served two
    thirds of the portion of the sentence --- including the additional
    sentence --- that begins on the day on which they are recommitted
    and ends on the day on which the sentence expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If additional sentence

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} If an offender receives an additional sentence for
  an offence under an Act of Parliament and their parole or statutory
  release is not revoked, their statutory release date is the day on
  which they have served, from the earlier of the day on which they are
  recommitted to custody as a result of the suspension of their parole
  or statutory release and the day on which they are recommitted to
  custody as a result of the additional sentence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any time remaining before the statutory release
    date in respect of the sentence they are serving when the additional
    sentence is imposed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two thirds of the period that equals the difference
    between the length of the sentence that includes the additional
    sentence and the length of the sentence that they are serving when
    the additional sentence is imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to earn and forfeiture of
  remission

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender receives a sentence to be served in
  a provincial correctional facility and fails to earn or forfeits any
  remission under the [Prisons and Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20)
  and is transferred to penitentiary, otherwise than pursuant to an
  agreement entered into under paragraph 16(1)(b), the offender is not
  entitled to be released until the day on which the offender has served

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period of imprisonment that the offender would
    have been required to serve under this section if the offender had
    not failed to earn or had not forfeited the remission; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of imprisonment equal to the remission
    that the offender failed to earn or forfeited and that was not
    recredited under that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervision after release

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An offender sentenced, committed or transferred
  (otherwise than pursuant to an agreement entered into under subsection
  16(1)) to penitentiary on or after August 1, 1970 who is released on
  statutory release is subject to supervision in accordance with this
  Act, but no other offender released under this section is subject to
  supervision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 127
- 1995, c. 42, s. 41
- 1999, c. 31, s. 66(E)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 81
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Youth Criminal Justice
Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5)

**[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this
Act, a young person in respect of whom a youth sentence is imposed under
paragraph 42(2)(n), (o), (q) or (r) of the [Youth Criminal Justice
Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5) and who is transferred to a penitentiary under
subsection 89(2), 92(2) or 93(2) of that Act is entitled to be released
from the penitentiary by virtue of statutory release on the day on which
the custodial portion of their youth sentence would have expired.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 82
:::

### [Effect of Parole, Statutory Release or Unescorted Temporary Absence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107094 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of sentence

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  offender who is released on parole, statutory release or unescorted
  temporary absence continues, while entitled to be at large, to serve
  the sentence until its expiration according to law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Freedom to be at large

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except to the extent required by the conditions of
  any day parole, an offender who is released on parole, statutory
  release or unescorted temporary absence is entitled, subject to this
  Part, to remain at large in accordance with the conditions of the
  parole, statutory release or unescorted temporary absence and is not
  liable to be returned to custody by reason of the sentence unless the
  parole, statutory release or unescorted temporary absence is
  suspended, cancelled, terminated or revoked.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence deemed to be completed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), for the purposes of paragraph
  50(b) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5)
  and section 64 of the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01), the
  sentence of an offender who has been released on parole, statutory
  release or an unescorted temporary absence is deemed to be completed
  unless the parole or statutory release has been suspended, terminated
  or revoked, the unescorted temporary absence is suspended or cancelled
  or the offender has returned to Canada before the expiration of the
  sentence according to law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite this Act, the [Prisons and Reformatories
  Act](/eng/acts/P-20) and the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), an
  offender against whom a removal order has been made under the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) is not
  eligible for day parole or an unescorted temporary absence until they
  are eligible for full parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parole inoperative where parole
  eligibility date in future

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, before the full parole eligibility date, a
  removal order is made under the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) against an offender who has received day parole
  or an unescorted temporary absence, on the day that the removal order
  is made, the day parole or unescorted temporary absence becomes
  inoperative and the offender shall be reincarcerated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An offender referred to in subsection (4) is eligible
  for day parole or an unescorted temporary absence if the removal order
  is stayed under paragraph 50(a), 66(b) or 114(1)(b) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where the removal order of an offender referred to in
  subsection (5) is stayed under paragraph 50(a), 66(b) or 114(1)(b) of
  the [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) on a day
  prior to the full parole eligibility of the offender, the unescorted
  temporary absence or day parole of that offender is resumed as of the
  day of the stay.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 128
- 1995, c. 42, ss. 42, 69(E), 71(F)
- 1999, c. 18, s. 87
- 2001, c. 27, s. 242
- 2012, c. 1, s. 83
:::
