    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any of the conditions attached to the offender's
      unescorted temporary absence, parole or statutory release and the
      reasons for any unescorted temporary absence,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the destination of the offender when released on
      unescorted temporary absence, parole or statutory release, and
      whether the offender will be in the vicinity of the victim while
      travelling to that destination,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} whether the offender is in custody and, if not,
      the reason that the offender is not in custody,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} whether or not the offender has appealed a
      decision of the Board under section 147, and the outcome of that
      appeal, and

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} the reason for a waiver of the right to a
      hearing under subsection 140(1) if the offender gives one.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender has been transferred from a
  penitentiary to a provincial correctional facility, the Chairperson of
  the Board may, at the request of a victim of an offence committed by
  the offender, disclose to the victim the name of the province in which
  the provincial facility is located if in the Chairperson's opinion the
  interest of the victim in such disclosure clearly outweighs any
  invasion of the offender's privacy that could result from the
  disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information to other
  persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) also apply, with such
  modifications as the circumstances require, to a person who satisfies
  the Chairperson

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that person suffered physical or emotional harm,
    property damage or economic loss, as the result of an act of an
    offender, whether or not the offender was prosecuted or convicted
    for that act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that a complaint was made to the police or the
    Crown attorney, or an information was laid under the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in respect of that act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A victim may designate a representative to whom the
  information referred to in subsections (1) and (2) is to be disclosed
  on the victim's behalf. In that case, the victim shall provide the
  Chairperson with the representative's contact information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of request

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} A victim who has made a request referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) may inform the Chairperson in writing that they
  no longer want the information to be disclosed to them. In that case,
  the Chairperson shall not contact them or their representative, if
  any, unless the victim subsequently makes the request again.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed withdrawal of request

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may consider a victim to have
  withdrawn a request referred to in subsection (1) or (2) if the
  Chairperson has made reasonable efforts to contact the victim and has
  failed to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other persons

  [(3.4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (3.1) to (3.3) also apply, with any
  necessary modifications, to a person who has satisfied the Chairperson
  of the matters referred to in paragraphs (3)(a) and (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The manner and form of making requests to the
  Chairperson under subsection (1) or (2), and how those requests are to
  be dealt with, may be provided for by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation by Chairperson

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, "Chairperson" includes a person or
  class of persons designated, by name or by position, by the
  Chairperson.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 142
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, ss. 57, 71(F), 72(F)
- 1997, c. 17, s. 35
- 2012, c. 1, s. 98
- 2015, c. 13, s. 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20120613.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Records of Reviews and Decisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107635 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records of proceedings

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board conducts a review of
  the case of an offender by way of a hearing, it shall maintain a
  record of the proceedings for the period prescribed by the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions to be recorded and
  communicated

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board renders a decision with respect to an
  offender following a review of the offender's case, it shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} record the decision and the reasons for the
    decision, and maintain a copy of the decision and reasons for the
    period prescribed by the regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the offender with a copy of the decision
    and the reasons for the decision, in whichever of the two official
    languages of Canada is requested by the offender, within the period
    prescribed by the regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registry of decisions

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board shall maintain a registry of the decisions rendered by it under
  this Part or under paragraph 746.1(2)(c) or (3)(c) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) and its reasons for those decisions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to registry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who demonstrates an interest in a case may,
  on written application to the Board, have access to the contents of
  the registry relating to that case, other than information the
  disclosure of which could reasonably be expected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to jeopardize the safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to reveal a source of information obtained in
    confidence; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if released publicly, to adversely affect the
    reintegration of the offender into society.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any conditions prescribed by the
  regulations, any person may have access for research purposes to the
  contents of the registry, other than the name of any person,
  information that could be used to identify any person or information
  the disclosure of which could jeopardize any person's safety.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2), where any information
  contained in a decision in the registry has been considered in the
  course of a hearing held in the presence of observers, any person may,
  on application in writing, have access to that information in the
  registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 144
- 2012, c. 1, s. 99
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-144-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 144"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of decision

**[[144.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144.1 .sectionLabel}** At the request of a
victim, or a person referred to in subsection 142(3), the Board shall,
despite section 144, provide the victim or person with a copy of any
decision rendered by it under this Part or under paragraph 746.1(2)(c)
or (3)(c) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in relation to the
offender and its reasons for that decision, unless doing so could
reasonably be expected

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to jeopardize the safety of any person;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to reveal a source of information obtained in
  confidence; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to prevent the successful reintegration of the
  offender into society.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 13, s. 51
:::

### [Review and Evidence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107664 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents admissible

**[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** A decision, order,
warrant or certificate purporting to be signed by a member of the Board
or a person designated by the Chairperson of the Board is admissible in
any court and is evidence of its contents without proof of the signature
or official character of the person appearing to have signed it.

### [Organization of the Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107667 .Subheading}

#### [Appeal Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-107668 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constitution of Appeal Division

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be a division of the
  Board known as the Appeal Division, consisting of not more than six
  full-time members --- one of whom shall be designated
  Vice-Chairperson, Appeal Division --- and a number of part-time
  members designated in both cases by the Governor in Council, on the
  recommendation of the Minister, from among the members appointed under
  section 103.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disqualification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the Appeal Division may not sit on an
  appeal from a decision in which the member participated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A member of a panel of the Appeal Division that
  orders a new review of a case pursuant to subsection 147(4) may not
  sit on the panel of the Board that reviews the case or on a panel of
  the Appeal Division that subsequently reviews the case on an appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 146
- 2012, c. 1, s. 100
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appeal to Appeal Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-107678 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  offender may appeal a decision of the Board to the Appeal Division on
  the ground that the Board, in making its decision,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} failed to observe a principle of fundamental
    justice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} made an error of law;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} breached or failed to apply a policy adopted
    pursuant to subsection 151(2);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} based its decision on erroneous or incomplete
    information; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} acted without jurisdiction or beyond its
    jurisdiction, or failed to exercise its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of Vice-Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Vice-Chairperson, Appeal Division, may refuse to
  hear an appeal, without causing a full review of the case to be
  undertaken, where, in the opinion of the Vice-Chairperson,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the appeal is frivolous or vexatious;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the relief sought is beyond the jurisdiction of the
    Board;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the appeal is based on information or on a new
    parole or statutory release plan that was not before the Board when
    it rendered the decision appealed from; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} at the time the notice of appeal is received by the
    Appeal Division, the offender has ninety days or less to serve
    before being released from imprisonment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and manner of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The time within which and the manner in which a
  decision of the Board may be appealed shall be as prescribed by the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision on appeal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Appeal Division, on the completion of a review of
  a decision appealed from, may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affirm the decision;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} affirm the decision but order a further review of
    the case by the Board on a date earlier than the date otherwise
    provided for the next review;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order a new review of the case by the Board and
    order the continuation of the decision pending the review; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} reverse, cancel or vary the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of immediate release

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Appeal Division shall not render a decision under
  subsection (4) that results in the immediate release of an offender
  from imprisonment unless it is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the decision appealed from cannot reasonably be
    supported in law, under the applicable policies of the Board, or on
    the basis of the information available to the Board in its review of
    the case; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a delay in releasing the offender from imprisonment
    would be unfair.

#### [Head Office and Regions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-107705 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  head office of the Board shall be located in the National Capital
  Region as described in the schedule to the [National Capital
  Act](/eng/acts/N-4), but meetings of the Board or of the Executive
  Committee of the Board may be held at such times and places as the
  Chairperson of the Board directs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regional offices

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall maintain at least one regional office
  at a place determined by the Chairperson, after consultation with the
  Minister, in each of the following regions of Canada, namely, the
  Atlantic region, Quebec, Ontario, the Prairie region and the Pacific
  region.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regional divisions

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be regional divisions of
  the Board consisting of the members assigned to them, who shall
  exercise such functions of the Board, under this or any other Act of
  Parliament, as are designated by the Chairperson of the Board for a
  region or, where there is more than one regional office in a region,
  for the portion of a region designated by the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Full-time members of the Board assigned to a regional
  division pursuant to subsection 105(3) shall reside within reasonable
  commuting distance of the office of that division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any act or thing done or any decision rendered by a
  panel of the Board constituted pursuant to subsection 105(6) is, for
  the purposes of this Part, an act or thing done or a decision rendered
  by the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vice-Chairpersons

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  full-time member shall be designated by the Governor in Council, on
  the recommendation of the Minister, to be Vice-Chairperson for each
  regional division of the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Vice-Chairperson for a division is responsible to
  the Chairperson for the professional conduct, training, and quality of
  decision-making of Board members assigned to that division.

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-107723 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Executive Committee

- **[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be an Executive
  Committee of the Board consisting of the Chairperson, the Executive
  Vice-Chairperson, the Vice-Chairperson, Appeal Division, the regional
  Vice-Chairpersons and two other members of the Board designated by the
  Chairperson after consultation with the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Executive Committee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall, after such consultation with Board members
    as it considers appropriate, adopt policies relating to reviews
    under this Part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall, where requested by the Chairperson, advise
    the Chairperson on any other matters concerning the functions of the
    Board or of the Chairperson under this or any other Act of
    Parliament; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may direct that the number of members required to
    constitute a panel for the review of any class of cases shall be
    greater than the number fixed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respect for diversity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Policies adopted under paragraph (2)(a) must respect
  gender, ethnic, cultural and linguistic differences and be responsive
  to the special needs of women and of Indigenous persons, as well as to
  the needs of other groups of offenders with special requirements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chair

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Meetings of the Executive Committee shall be chaired
  by the Chairperson.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 151
- 1995, c. 42, s. 58(F)
- [2019, c. 27, s. 35](#1151593-1171060){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151593-1171060.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-151-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 151"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Executive Officer

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson of the Board is its chief executive officer and as such
  has supervision over and direction of the work and the staff of the
  Board, and the Chairperson shall chair general meetings of the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of member

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may direct that a member of the Board
  not participate in a review panel where, in the opinion of the
  Chairperson, the participation of the member in the review may result
  in a reasonable apprehension of bias.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constitution of review panels

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may direct that the number of members
  required to constitute a panel for the review of any particular case
  shall be greater than the number fixed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may appoint a person or persons to
  investigate and report on any matter relating to the operations of the
  Board, and sections 7 to 13 of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11)
  apply in respect of such investigations, with such modifications as
  the circumstances require, as if the references to "commissioners" in
  those sections were references to the person or persons so appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may authorize any full-time member of
  the Board to exercise any of the Chairperson's functions under this
  Part, in accordance with any conditions specified by the Chairperson,
  and a function so exercised shall be deemed to have been exercised by
  the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of exercising

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where the Chairperson is authorized by this Part to
  designate a person to exercise a power, the Chairperson may specify
  the conditions under which that person may exercise the power.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence, incapacity or vacancy

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  Chairperson or a vacancy in the office of Chairperson, the Executive
  Vice-Chairperson may exercise all the powers of the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of, or a
  vacancy in the offices of, the Chairperson and the Executive
  Vice-Chairperson, a full-time member of the Board designated by the
  Minister may exercise all the powers of the Chairperson.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration of full-time and substitute
members

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  full-time and substitute member of the Board shall be paid such
  remuneration as is fixed by the Governor in Council, and is entitled
  to be paid reasonable travel and living expenses incurred while
  performing duties away from the administrative centre to which the
  member is assigned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave of absence from public service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee in the public service appointed as a
  full-time member of the Board shall be given leave of absence without
  pay from the public service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration of part-time members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each part-time member of the Board shall be paid such
  remuneration as is fixed by the Governor in Council for each day that
  the member is serving as such, and is entitled to be paid reasonable
  travel and living expenses incurred while performing duties away from
  the member's ordinary place of residence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The full-time members and employees of the Board
  shall be deemed to be employed in the public service for the purposes
  of the [Public Service Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 153
- 2003, c. 22, s. 225(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-153-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 153"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity of members

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** No criminal or civil
proceedings lie against a member of the Board for anything done or said
in good faith in the exercise or purported exercise of the functions of
a member of the Board under this or any other Act of Parliament.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board members not to be witnesses

**[[154.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154.1 .sectionLabel}** A member of the
Board is not a competent or compellable witness in any civil proceedings
in respect of any matter coming to their knowledge in the course of the
exercise or purported exercise of their functions under this or any
other Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 101
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Impartiality

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  full-time member of the Board shall not hold any office or engage in
  any occupation incompatible with the exercise of the member's
  functions under this or any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the Board may not participate in a review
  of a case in circumstances where a reasonable apprehension of bias may
  result from the participation of that member.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiries

- **[[155.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may recommend to the
  Minister that an inquiry be held to determine whether any member of
  the Board should be subject to any disciplinary or remedial measures
  for any reason set out in any of paragraphs 155.2(2)(a) to (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judge to conduct inquiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister considers it appropriate that an
  inquiry under this section be held, a judge, supernumerary judge or
  former judge of the Federal Court of Canada, the Federal Court of
  Appeal or the Federal Court, in this section and section 155.2
  referred to as a "judge", shall conduct the inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge conducting an inquiry under this section has
  all the powers, rights and privileges that are vested in a superior
  court and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing, has
  the power

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to issue to any person a summons requiring the
    person to appear at the time and place mentioned in the summons to
    testify with respect to all matters within the person's knowledge
    relative to the inquiry and to bring and produce any document, book
    or paper that the person has or controls relative to the inquiry;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths and examine any person on oath.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry public

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) and (6), an inquiry under
  this section shall be conducted in public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A judge conducting an inquiry under this section may,
  on application, take any measures or make any order that the judge
  considers necessary to ensure the confidentiality of the inquiry where
  the judge is satisfied that, during the inquiry or as a result of the
  inquiry being conducted in public, as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} matters involving public security may be disclosed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} financial or personal or other matters may be
    disclosed of such a nature that the desirability of avoiding public
    disclosures of those matters in the interest of any person affected
    or in the public interest outweighs the desirability of adhering to
    the principle that the inquiry be conducted in public; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a reasonable likelihood that the life,
    liberty or security of a person would be endangered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a judge conducting an inquiry under this
  section considers it appropriate to do so, the judge may take any
  measures or make any order that the judge considers necessary to
  ensure the confidentiality of any hearing held in respect of an
  application referred to in subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of evidence

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A judge conducting an inquiry under this section is
  not bound by any legal or technical rules of evidence and, in any
  proceedings of the inquiry, the judge may receive and base a decision
  on evidence adduced in the proceedings and considered credible or
  trustworthy in the circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to grant standing

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A judge conducting an inquiry under this section may
  grant standing to the hearing to any party where the judge determines
  such an order to be appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be heard

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every person in respect of whom an inquiry under this
  section is conducted shall be given reasonable notice of the
  subject-matter of the inquiry and of the time and place of any hearing
  thereof and shall be given an opportunity, in person or by counsel, to
  be heard at the hearing, to cross-examine witnesses and to adduce
  evidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 42, s. 59
- 2002, c. 8, s. 132
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-155.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 155.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of inquiry

- **[[155.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After an inquiry under section 155.1
  has been completed, the judge who conducted the inquiry shall prepare
  a report of the conclusions of the inquiry and submit it to the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an inquiry under section 155.1 has been held
  and, in the opinion of the judge who conducted the inquiry, the member
  of the Board in respect of whom the inquiry was held

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has become incapacitated from the due execution of
    the member's office by reason of infirmity,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of misconduct,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has failed in the due execution of the member's
    office, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} has been placed, by conduct or otherwise, in a
    position that is incompatible with the due execution of the member's
    office,

  the judge may, in the report of the inquiry, recommend that the member
  be suspended without pay or be removed from office or may recommend
  that such disciplinary or remedial measure as the judge considers
  necessary be taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council may suspend or
  remove

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister receives a report under subsection
  (1), the Minister shall send a copy of the report to the Governor in
  Council, who may suspend the member of the Board to whom the report
  relates without pay, remove the member from office or take any other
  disciplinary or remedial measure.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 42, s. 59
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107817 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations providing for anything that
  by this Part is to be provided for by regulation, including defining
  terms that are to be defined in the regulations for the purposes of
  this Part, and, generally, for carrying out the purposes and
  provisions of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations may be made pursuant to subsection (1)
  that are applicable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of offenders within the jurisdiction of
    a provincial parole board; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a specified class, or specified
    classes, of offenders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by regulation, amend
  Schedule I or II.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  respecting the method of determining

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to sections 120 to 120.3, the period that
    an offender must serve before being eligible for parole;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to section 127, the period that an
    offender must serve before being entitled to statutory release; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which subsection 139(1) applies in
    respect of sentences.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 156
- 1995, c. 42, s. 60
- 2015, c. 30, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-156-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 156"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Correctional Investigator]{.HTitleText1} {#h-107834 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107835 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
    I; ([commissaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Correctional Investigator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Correctional Investigator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    Correctional Investigator of Canada appointed pursuant to section
    158; ([enquêteur correctionnel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[long-term supervision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
    as in Part I; ([surveillance de longue durée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
    I; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offender]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
    II; ([délinquant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[parole]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
    II; ([libération conditionnelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in Part
    I; ([pénitencier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[provincial parole board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
    as in Part II; ([commission provinciale]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[statutory release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    Part II. ([libération d'office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 157
- 1997, c. 17, s. 36
- 2005, c. 10, s. 17(F)
- 2012, c. 1, s. 102
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-157-20050404.html "Link to previous version of section 157"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to persons subject to
long-term supervision order

**[[157.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157.1 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
required to be supervised by a long-term supervision order is deemed to
be an offender for the purposes of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 17, s. 37
:::

### [Correctional Investigator]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107853 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may appoint a person to be known as the Correctional Investigator of
Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** A person is eligible to
be appointed as Correctional Investigator or to continue in that office
only if the person is a Canadian citizen ordinarily resident in Canada
or a permanent resident within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
[Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) who is
ordinarily resident in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 159
- 2001, c. 27, s. 243
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office and removal

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Correctional Investigator holds office during good behaviour for a
  term not exceeding five years, but may be suspended or removed for
  cause at any time by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further terms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator, on the expiration of a
  first or any subsequent term of office, is eligible to be re-appointed
  for a further term.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence, incapacity or vacancy

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** In the event of the
absence or incapacity of the Correctional Investigator, or if the office
of Correctional Investigator is vacant, the Governor in Council may
appoint another qualified person to hold office instead of the
Correctional Investigator during the absence, incapacity or vacancy, and
that person shall, while holding that office, have the same function as
and all of the powers and duties of the Correctional Investigator under
this Part and be paid such salary or other remuneration and expenses as
may be fixed by the Governor in Council.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Devotion to duties

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator shall engage exclusively in the function and duties of the
office of the Correctional Investigator and shall not hold any other
office under Her Majesty in right of Canada or a province for reward or
engage in any other employment for reward.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Salary and expenses

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Correctional Investigator shall be paid such salary as may be fixed by
  the Governor in Council and is entitled to be paid reasonable travel
  and living expenses incurred in the performance of duties under this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pension benefits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36), other than those relating to tenure of office,
  apply to the Correctional Investigator, except that a person appointed
  as Correctional Investigator from outside the public service, as
  defined in subsection 3(1) of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36), may, by notice in writing given to the President
  of the Treasury Board not more than sixty days after the date of
  appointment, elect to participate in the pension plan provided for in
  the [Diplomatic Service (Special) Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/D-2),
  in which case the provisions of that Act, other than those relating to
  tenure of office, apply to the Correctional Investigator from the date
  of appointment and the provisions of the [Public Service
  Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36) do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other benefits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator is deemed to be
  employed in the federal public administration for the purposes of the
  [Government Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and any
  regulations made under section 9 of the [Aeronautics
  Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 163
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 224(E), 225(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 163"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Management]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107878 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator has the control and management of all matters connected
with the office of the Correctional Investigator.

### [Staff]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107881 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff of the Correctional Investigator

- **[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Such
  officers and employees as are necessary to enable the Correctional
  Investigator to perform the function and duties of the Correctional
  Investigator under this Part shall be appointed in accordance with the
  [Public Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technical assistance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator may engage on a
  temporary basis the services of persons having technical or
  specialized knowledge of any matter relating to the work of the
  Correctional Investigator to advise and assist the Correctional
  Investigator in the performance of the function and duties of the
  Correctional Investigator under this Part and, with the approval of
  the Treasury Board, may fix and pay the remuneration and expenses of
  those persons.

### [Oath of Office]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107887 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of office

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator and every person appointed pursuant to section 161 or
subsection 165(1) shall, before commencing the duties of office, take
the following oath of office:

"I, (name), swear that I will faithfully and impartially to the best of
my abilities perform the duties required of me as (Correctional
Investigator, Acting Correctional Investigator or officer or employee of
the Correctional Investigator). So help me God."

### [Function]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107890 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Function

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is the function of the Correctional Investigator to conduct
  investigations into the problems of offenders related to decisions,
  recommendations, acts or omissions of the Commissioner or any person
  under the control and management of, or performing services for or on
  behalf of, the Commissioner that affect offenders either individually
  or as a group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In performing the function referred to in subsection
  (1), the Correctional Investigator may not investigate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any decision, recommendation, act or omission of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Parole Board of Canada in the exercise of its
      exclusive jurisdiction under this Act, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any provincial parole board in the exercise of
      its exclusive jurisdiction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any problem of an offender related to the
    offender's confinement in a provincial correctional facility,
    whether or not the confinement is pursuant to an agreement between
    the federal government and the government of the province in which
    the provincial correctional facility is located; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any decision, recommendation, act or omission of an
    official of a province supervising, pursuant to an agreement between
    the federal government and the government of the province, an
    offender on temporary absence, parole, statutory release subject to
    supervision or mandatory supervision where the matter has been, is
    being or is going to be investigated by an ombudsman of that
    province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding paragraph (2)(b), the Correctional
  Investigator may, in any province that has not appointed a provincial
  parole board, investigate the problems of offenders confined in
  provincial correctional facilities in that province related to the
  preparation of cases of parole by any person under the control and
  management of, or performing services for or on behalf of, the
  Commissioner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 167
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-167-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 167"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to Federal Court

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** Where any question
arises as to whether the Correctional Investigator has jurisdiction to
investigate any particular problem, the Correctional Investigator may
apply to the Federal Court for a declaratory order determining the
question.

### [Information Program]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107907 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information program

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator shall maintain a program of communicating information to
offenders concerning

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the function of the Correctional Investigator;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances under which an investigation may be
  commenced by the Correctional Investigator; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the independence of the Correctional Investigator.

### [Investigations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107913 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencement

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Correctional Investigator may commence an investigation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the receipt of a complaint by or on behalf of an
    offender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the request of the Minister; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on the initiative of the Correctional Investigator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discretion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator has full discretion as
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether an investigation should be conducted in
    relation to any particular complaint or request;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} how every investigation is to be carried out; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether any investigation should be terminated
    before its completion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to hold hearing

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the course of an investigation, the Correctional Investigator may hold
  any hearing and make such inquiries as the Correctional Investigator
  considers appropriate, but no person is entitled as of right to be
  heard by the Correctional Investigator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearings to be [in camera]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every hearing held by the Correctional Investigator
  shall be [in camera]{.otherLang lang="la"} unless the Correctional
  Investigator decides otherwise.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to require information and
documents

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the course of an investigation, the Correctional Investigator may
  require any person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to furnish any information that, in the opinion of
    the Correctional Investigator, the person may be able to furnish in
    relation to the matter being investigated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), to produce, for
    examination by the Correctional Investigator, any document, paper or
    thing that, in the opinion of the Correctional Investigator, relates
    to the matter being investigated and that may be in the possession
    or under the control of that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of document, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator shall return any
  document, paper or thing produced pursuant to paragraph (1)(b) to the
  person who produced it within ten days after a request therefor is
  made to the Correctional Investigator, but nothing in this subsection
  precludes the Correctional Investigator from again requiring its
  production in accordance with paragraph (1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to make copies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator may make copies of any
  document, paper or thing produced pursuant to paragraph (1)(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to examine under oath

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the course of an investigation, the Correctional Investigator may
  summon and examine on oath

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the investigation is in relation to a
    complaint, the complainant, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person who, in the opinion of the Correctional
    Investigator, is able to furnish any information relating to the
    matter being investigated,

  and for that purpose may administer an oath.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation by counsel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is summoned pursuant to subsection
  (1), that person may be represented by counsel during the examination
  in respect of which the person is summoned.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to enter

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, the Correctional Investigator may, on satisfying any
applicable security requirements, at any time enter any premises
occupied by or under the control and management of the Commissioner and
inspect the premises and carry out therein any investigation or
inspection.

### [Findings, Reports and Recommendations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107949 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision not to investigate

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** Where the Correctional
Investigator decides not to conduct an investigation in relation to a
complaint or a request from the Minister or decides to terminate such an
investigation before its completion, the Correctional Investigator shall
inform the complainant or the Minister, as the case may be, of that
decision and, if the Correctional Investigator considers it appropriate,
the reasons therefor, providing the complainant with only such
information as can be disclosed pursuant to the [Privacy
Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and the [Access to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint not substantiated

**[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** Where, after conducting
an investigation in relation to a complaint, the Correctional
Investigator concludes that the complaint has not been substantiated,
the Correctional Investigator shall inform the complainant of that
conclusion and, where the Correctional Investigator considers it
appropriate, the reasons therefor, providing the complainant with only
such information as can be disclosed pursuant to the [Privacy
Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and the [Access to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Informing of problem

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** Where, after conducting
an investigation, the Correctional Investigator determines that a
problem referred to in section 167 exists in relation to one or more
offenders, the Correctional Investigator shall inform

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Commissioner, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the problem arises out of the exercise of a
  power delegated by the Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada to a
  person under the control and management of the Commissioner, the
  Commissioner and the Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada

of the problem and the particulars thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 177
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-177-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 177"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opinion re decision, recommendation, etc.

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, after conducting an
  investigation, the Correctional Investigator is of the opinion that
  the decision, recommendation, act or omission to which a problem
  referred to in section 167 relates

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appears to have been contrary to law or to an
    established policy,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was unreasonable, unjust, oppressive or improperly
    discriminatory, or was in accordance with a rule of law or a
    provision of any Act or a practice or policy that is or may be
    unreasonable, unjust, oppressive or improperly discriminatory, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} was based wholly or partly on a mistake of law or
    fact,

  the Correctional Investigator shall indicate that opinion, and the
  reasons therefor, when informing the Commissioner, or the Commissioner
  and the Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada, as the case may be,
  of the problem.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opinion re exercise of discretionary
  power

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, after conducting an investigation, the
  Correctional Investigator is of the opinion that in the making of the
  decision or recommendation, or in the act or omission, to which a
  problem referred to in section 167 relates a discretionary power has
  been exercised

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for an improper purpose,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on irrelevant grounds,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on the taking into account of irrelevant
    considerations, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} without reasons having been given,

  the Correctional Investigator shall indicate that opinion, and the
  reasons therefor, when informing the Commissioner, or the Commissioner
  and the Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada, as the case may be,
  of the problem.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 178
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-178-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 178"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  informing the Commissioner, or the Commissioner and the Chairperson of
  the Parole Board of Canada, as the case may be, of a problem, the
  Correctional Investigator may make any recommendation that the
  Correctional Investigator considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations in relation to
  decision, recommendation, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In making recommendations in relation to a decision,
  recommendation, act or omission referred to in subsection 167(1), the
  Correctional Investigator may, without restricting the generality of
  subsection (1), recommend that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reasons be given to explain why the decision or
    recommendation was made or the act or omission occurred;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the decision, recommendation, act or omission be
    referred to the appropriate authority for further consideration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the decision or recommendation be cancelled or
    varied;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the act or omission be rectified; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the law, practice or policy on which the decision,
    recommendation, act or omission was based be altered or
    reconsidered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations not binding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Neither the Commissioner nor the Chairperson of the
  Parole Board of Canada is bound to act on any finding or
  recommendation made under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 179
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice and report to Minister

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** If, within a reasonable
time after informing the Commissioner, or the Commissioner and the
Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada, as the case may be, of a
problem, no action is taken that seems to the Correctional Investigator
to be adequate and appropriate, the Correctional Investigator shall
inform the Minister of that fact and provide the Minister with whatever
information was originally provided to the Commissioner, or the
Commissioner and the Chairperson of the Parole Board of Canada, as the
case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 180
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-180-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 180"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complainant to be informed of result of
investigation

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** Where an investigation
is in relation to a complaint, the Correctional Investigator shall, in
such manner and at such time as the Correctional Investigator considers
appropriate, inform the complainant of the results of the investigation,
providing the complainant with only such information as can be disclosed
pursuant to the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and the [Access to
Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1).

### [Confidentiality]{.HTitleText2} {#h-107997 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

**[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this Part,
the Correctional Investigator and every person acting on behalf or under
the direction of the Correctional Investigator shall not disclose any
information that comes to their knowledge in the exercise of their
powers or the performance of their functions and duties under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure authorized

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  Correctional Investigator may disclose or may authorize any person
  acting on behalf or under the direction of the Correctional
  Investigator to disclose information

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that, in the opinion of the Correctional
    Investigator, is necessary to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} carry out an investigation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} establish the grounds for findings and
      recommendations made under this Part; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the course of a prosecution for an offence under
    this Part or a prosecution for an offence under section 131
    (perjury) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in respect of a
    statement made under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Correctional Investigator and every person acting
  on behalf or under the direction of the Correctional Investigator
  shall take every reasonable precaution to avoid the disclosure of, and
  shall not disclose, any information the disclosure of which could
  reasonably be expected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to disclose information obtained or prepared in the
    course of lawful investigations pertaining to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the detection, prevention or suppression of
      crime,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of any law of Canada or a
      province, where the investigation is ongoing, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} activities suspected of constituting threats to
      the security of Canada within the meaning of the [Canadian
      Security Intelligence Service Act](/eng/acts/C-23),

    if the information came into existence less than twenty years before
    the anticipated disclosure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to be injurious to the conduct of any lawful
    investigation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any individual under sentence for an
    offence against any Act of Parliament, to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} lead to a serious disruption of that individual's
      institutional or conditional release program, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} result in physical or other harm to that
      individual or any other person;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to disclose advice or recommendations developed by
    or for a government institution within the meaning of the [Access to
    Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1) or a minister of the Crown; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to disclose confidences of the Queen's Privy
    Council for Canada referred to in section 196.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[investigation]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(b),
  [[investigation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an investigation that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pertains to the administration or enforcement of an
    Act of Parliament or of a province; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is authorized by or pursuant to an Act of
    Parliament or of a province.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Letters to be unopened

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding any
provision in any Act or regulation, where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a letter written by an offender is addressed to the
  Correctional Investigator, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a letter written by the Correctional Investigator is
  addressed to an offender,

the letter shall immediately be forwarded unopened to the Correctional
Investigator or to the offender, as the case may be, by the person in
charge of the institution at which the offender is incarcerated.

### [Delegation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108029 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation by Correctional Investigator

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Correctional Investigator may authorize any person to exercise or
  perform, subject to such restrictions or limitations as the
  Correctional Investigator may specify, the function, powers and duties
  of the Correctional Investigator under this Part except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the power to delegate under this section; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the duty or power to make a report to the Minister
    under section 192 or 193.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation is revocable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every delegation under this section is revocable at
  will and no delegation prevents the exercise or performance by the
  Correctional Investigator of the delegated function, powers and
  duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing effect of delegation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the event that the Correctional Investigator who
  makes a delegation under this section ceases to hold office, the
  delegation continues in effect so long as the delegate continues in
  office or until revoked by a succeeding Correctional Investigator.

### [Relationship With Other Acts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108039 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to conduct investigations

- **[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  power of the Correctional Investigator to conduct investigations
  exists notwithstanding any provision in any Act to the effect that the
  matter being investigated is final and that no appeal lies in respect
  thereof or that the matter may not be challenged, reviewed, quashed or
  in any way called into question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relationship with other Acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The power of the Correctional Investigator to conduct
  investigations is in addition to the provisions of any other Act or
  rule of law under which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any remedy or right of appeal or objection is
    provided for any person, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any procedure is provided for the inquiry into or
    investigation of any matter,

  and nothing in this Part limits or affects any such remedy, right of
  appeal, objection or procedure.

### [Legal Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108048 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acts not to be questioned or subject to
review

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** Except on the ground of
lack of jurisdiction, nothing done by the Correctional Investigator,
including the making of any report or recommendation, is liable to be
challenged, reviewed, quashed or called into question in any court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of Correctional Investigator

**[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** No criminal or civil
proceedings lie against the Correctional Investigator, or against any
person acting on behalf or under the direction of the Correctional
Investigator, for anything done, reported or said in good faith in the
course of the exercise or performance or purported exercise or
performance of any function, power or duty of the Correctional
Investigator.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No summons

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator or any person acting on behalf or under the direction of
the Correctional Investigator is not a competent or compellable witness
in respect of any matter coming to the knowledge of the Correctional
Investigator or that person in the course of the exercise or performance
or purported exercise or performance of any function, power or duty of
the Correctional Investigator, in any proceedings other than a
prosecution for an offence under this Part or a prosecution for an
offence under section 131 (perjury) of the [Criminal
Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in respect of a statement made under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Libel or slander

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of any
law relating to libel or slander,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} anything said, any information furnished or any
  document, paper or thing produced in good faith in the course of an
  investigation by or on behalf of the Correctional Investigator under
  this Part is privileged; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any report made in good faith by the Correctional
  Investigator under this Part and any fair and accurate account of the
  report made in good faith in a newspaper or any other periodical
  publication or in a broadcast is privileged.

### [Offence and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108059 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} without lawful justification or excuse, wilfully
  obstructs, hinders or resists the Correctional Investigator or any
  other person in the exercise or performance of the function, powers or
  duties of the Correctional Investigator,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} without lawful justification or excuse, refuses or
  wilfully fails to comply with any lawful requirement of the
  Correctional Investigator or any other person under this Part, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} wilfully makes any false statement to or misleads or
  attempts to mislead the Correctional Investigator or any other person
  in the exercise or performance of the function, powers or duties of
  the Correctional Investigator

is guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction and liable to a
fine not exceeding two thousand dollars.

### [Annual and Special Reports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108066 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual reports

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator shall, within three months after the end of each fiscal
year, submit to the Minister a report of the activities of the office of
the Correctional Investigator during that year, and the Minister shall
cause every such report to be laid before each House of Parliament on
any of the first thirty days on which that House is sitting after the
day on which the Minister receives it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Urgent matters

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** The Correctional
Investigator may, at any time, make a special report to the Minister
referring to and commenting on any matter within the scope of the
function, powers and duties of the Correctional Investigator where, in
the opinion of the Correctional Investigator, the matter is of such
urgency or importance that a report thereon should not be deferred until
the time provided for the submission of the next annual report to the
Minister under section 192, and the Minister shall cause every such
special report to be laid before each House of Parliament on any of the
first thirty days on which that House is sitting after the day on which
the Minister receives it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting of public hearings

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** Where the Correctional
Investigator decides to hold hearings in public in relation to any
investigation, the Correctional Investigator shall indicate in relation
to that investigation, in the report submitted under section 192, the
reasons why the hearings were held in public.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adverse comments

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** Where it appears to the
Correctional Investigator that there may be sufficient grounds for
including in a report under section 192 or 193 any comment or
information that reflects or might reflect adversely on any person or
organization, the Correctional Investigator shall give that person or
organization a reasonable opportunity to make representations respecting
the comment or information and shall include in the report a fair and
accurate summary of those representations.

### [Confidences of the Queen's Privy Council]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108075 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidences of the Queen's Privy Council
for Canada

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  powers of the Correctional Investigator under sections 172, 173 and
  174 do not apply with respect to confidences of the Queen's Privy
  Council for Canada, including, without restricting the generality of
  the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} memoranda the purpose of which is to present
    proposals or recommendations to Council;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discussion papers the purpose of which is to
    present background explanations, analyses of problems or policy
    options to Council for consideration by Council in making decisions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} agenda of Council or records recording
    deliberations or decisions of Council;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} records used for or reflecting communications or
    discussions between ministers of the Crown on matters relating to
    the making of government decisions or the formulation of government
    policy;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records the purpose of which is to brief ministers
    of the Crown in relation to matters that are before, or are proposed
    to be brought before, Council or that are the subject of
    communications or discussions referred to in paragraph (d);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} draft legislation; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} records that contain information about the contents
    of any record within a class of records referred to in
    paragraphs (a) to (f).

- Definition of [[Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1),
  [[Council]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Queen's Privy Council for
  Canada, committees of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada, Cabinet
  and committees of Cabinet.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confidences of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada
    that have been in existence for more than twenty years; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discussion papers described in paragraph (1)(b)

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the decisions to which the discussion papers
      relate have been made public, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the decisions have not been made public,
      if four years have passed since the decisions were made.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108094 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make such regulations as the Governor in Council deems necessary for
carrying out the purposes and provisions of this Part.

### [Her Majesty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108097 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on Her Majesty

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** This Part is binding on
Her Majesty in right of Canada.

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Consequential and Related Amendments, Repeal and Transitional Provisions and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-108100 .Part}

### [Criminal Code]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108101 .Subheading}

**[[199 to 203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199_to_203
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1995, c.
42, s. 61\]]{.Repealed}

### [Prisons and Reformatories Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108104 .Subheading}

**[[205 to 207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205_to_207
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

### [Transfer of Offenders Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108106 .Subheading}

**[[208 to 211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208_to_211
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

### [Department of the Solicitor General Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108108 .Subheading}

.sectionLabel}** \[Repeals\]

### [References]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108112 .Subheading}
