.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

### [Transitional Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108114 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner remains in office

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** The person holding
office as Commissioner of Corrections on the coming into force of
section 214 continues in office as Commissioner and shall be deemed to
have been appointed under Part I of this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal-provincial agreements

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  agreements made under the Penitentiary Act or the [Prisons and
  Reformatories Act](/eng/acts/P-20) and in existence on the coming into
  force of section 214, to the extent that they are authorized to be
  entered into under subsection 15(3) or 16(1) of this Act, shall be
  deemed to have been entered into under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any agreements made under the Parole Act and in
  existence on the coming into force of section 213, to the extent that
  they are authorized to be entered into under section 114 of this Act,
  shall be deemed to have been entered into under that section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary absences

**[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** Any temporary absence
authorized under section 28 or 29 of the Penitentiary Act shall, on the
coming into force of section 214, be dealt with as if it had been
authorized under section 17 of this Act.

**[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019, c.
27, s. 36\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-220-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 220"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** In this section and
sections 222 to 227,

[[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on which
    section 213 comes into force; ([entrée en vigueur]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Parole Act as it read
    immediately before the commencement day; ([loi
    antérieure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[former Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National Parole Board
    established by section 3 of the former Act. ([ancienne
    Commission]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of former Board

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who, immediately before the commencement day, was a full-time
  or temporary member of the former Board shall be deemed to have been
  appointed on the commencement day pursuant to section 103 to hold that
  office on the Board for a term equal to the remainder of the term for
  which the person was appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former Chairman and Vice-Chairman

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The persons who, immediately before the commencement
  day, were Chairman and Vice-Chairman of the former Board shall be
  deemed to have been designated on the commencement day pursuant to
  section 104 to hold the offices of Chairperson and Executive
  Vice-Chairperson, respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of community Board members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The repeal of the former Act does not affect the
  designation of a person who, immediately before the commencement day,
  was a person designated by the Minister pursuant to section 8 of the
  former Act, who may continue to conduct, as a regional community
  representative of the Board, the reviews of the classes of cases of
  offenders referred to in that section for the remainder of the term
  for which the person was designated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in subsection (3) shall be paid
  such remuneration as is fixed by the Governor in Council for each day
  that the person is performing duties referred to in that subsection,
  and is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and living expenses
  incurred while performing those duties away from the person's ordinary
  place of residence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reviews in progress

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** A review of the case of
an offender begun under the former Act shall be continued after the
commencement day as if it had been begun under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parole and temporary absences

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  parole granted or temporary absence authorized under the former Act
  shall, on and after the commencement day, be dealt with as if it had
  been granted or authorized under Part II of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory supervision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person who is at large and subject to mandatory
  supervision under the former Act immediately before the commencement
  day shall be deemed, after that day, to be on statutory release under
  Part II of this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Day parole eligibility of past offenders

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1.1),
  paragraph 119(1)(c) does not apply in respect of an offender who is
  serving a sentence imposed before November 1, 1992, but the
  corresponding provisions of the former Act and the regulations made
  under that Act apply in respect thereof as if they were provisions of
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where additional sentence

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 119(1)(c) applies in respect of an
  offender who is serving a sentence imposed before November 1, 1992
  where the offender receives an additional sentence on or after that
  day and, as a result, the offender is deemed, pursuant to section 139,
  to have been sentenced to one sentence.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2011, c. 11, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 225
- 1995, c. 42, s. 62
- 2011, c. 11, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-225-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 225"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of eligibility date for
parole

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender who was serving a
  sentence before the commencement day is sentenced, before that
  sentence expires and after the coming into force of section 743.6 of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), to another sentence for an
  offence referred to in that section that was prosecuted by way of
  indictment, and the court determines pursuant to that section that the
  offender shall serve one half of the sentence imposed by it, that
  offender may be released on full parole after having served a period
  of imprisonment equal to the lesser of one half or ten years of that
  other sentence and, in addition,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the two sentences are to be served
    concurrently, one third of any portion of the first sentence that is
    not served concurrently with the other sentence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the two sentences are to be served
    consecutively, the lesser of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one third of the first sentence, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the portion of the sentence that would have had
      to be served before full parole could have been granted in the
      event that the two sentences were to have been served
      concurrently.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No offender referred to in subsection (1) is required
  to serve more than one half of the offender's sentence before becoming
  eligible to be released on full parole.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 20, s. 226
- 1995, c. 22, s. 13, c. 42, ss. 69(E), 70(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of mandatory supervision

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** Where a person was
released before the commencement day subject to mandatory supervision
following an order referred to in section 26.1 of the Penitentiary Act
and the release subject to mandatory supervision is revoked under Part
II of this Act, the offender is not entitled to be released thereafter
on statutory release under that Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remission forfeited under Penitentiary
Act

**[[227.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.1 .sectionLabel}** Any remission that
was forfeited under subsection 25(6) of the Penitentiary Act, as that
Act read immediately before November 1, 1992, is deemed, as of November
1, 1992, to be recredited and the offender continues to be subject to
the order under subsection 21.4(4) of the Parole Act, as that Act read
immediately before November 1, 1992, as if the order had been made under
section 130 of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 42, s. 63
:::

**[[228 and 229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228_and_229
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correctional Investigator

**[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** The person holding
office as Correctional Investigator under the [Inquiries
Act](/eng/acts/I-11) immediately before the coming into force of this
section continues in office as Correctional Investigator and shall be
deemed to have been appointed under Part III of this Act for a term of
one year beginning on the coming into force of this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff of Correctional Investigator

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person whose services were engaged by the Correctional Investigator on
  a full-time basis pursuant to the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11)
  during any period immediately before the coming into force of this
  section shall be deemed to have been appointed in accordance with the
  [Public Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01) on the coming into
  force of this section, unless the person otherwise elects in writing
  within ninety days after the coming into force of this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Probation under [Public Service
  Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1) of this section and
  section 28 of the [Public Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01),
  a person who is deemed by subsection (1) of this section to have been
  appointed in accordance with the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not subject to probation under that Act if the
    person's services were engaged on a full-time basis by the
    Correctional Investigator during a period of at least one year
    immediately before the coming into force of this section; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is subject to probation under that Act for a period
    equal to one year minus the period during which the person's
    services were engaged on a full-time basis by the Correctional
    Investigator immediately before the coming into force of this
    section, where the latter period is less than one year.

### [Review of Detention Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108182 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of detention provisions after
three years

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Three years after the coming into
  force of sections 129 to 132, a comprehensive review of the operation
  of those sections shall be undertaken by such committee of the House
  of Commons as may be designated or established by the House of Commons
  for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to House of Commons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1) shall,
  within one year after a review is undertaken pursuant to that
  subsection or within such further time as the House of Commons may
  authorize, submit a report on the review to the House of Commons.

### [Review of Whole Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108188 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of whole Act

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Five
  years after the coming into force of this Act, a comprehensive review
  of the provisions and operation of this Act shall be undertaken by
  such committee of the House of Commons or of both Houses of Parliament
  as may be designated or established by Parliament for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1) shall,
  within a year after a review is undertaken pursuant to that subsection
  or within such further time as Parliament may authorize, submit a
  report to Parliament including a statement of any changes the
  committee recommends.

### [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-108194 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coming into force

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#C-44.6_en_1){#C-44.6_en_1-ID0EBCA}[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234
.sectionLabel}** This Act or any provision thereof shall come into force
on a day or days to be fixed by order of the Governor in Council.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#C-44.6_en_1-ID0EBCA){#C-44.6_en_1}\[Note: Act,
  except section 204, in force November 1, 1992, *see* SI/92-197;

## [SCHEDULE II]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 107(1), 129(1), (2) and (9), 130(3) and (4) and 156(3))]{.OriginatingRef} {#h-108326 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** An offence under any of the following provisions of the Narcotic
  Control Act, as it read immediately before the day on which section 64
  of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8) came
  into force, that was prosecuted by way of indictment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 4 (trafficking);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 5 (importing and exporting);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 6 (cultivation);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} section 19.1 (possession of property obtained by
    certain offences); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} section 19.2 (laundering proceeds of certain
    offences).

- **2** An offence under any of the following provisions of the [Food
  and Drugs Act](/eng/acts/F-27), as it read immediately before the day
  on which section 64 of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances
  Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8) came into force, that was prosecuted by way of
  indictment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 39 (trafficking in controlled drugs);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 44.2 (possession of property obtained by
    trafficking in controlled drugs);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 44.3 (laundering proceeds of trafficking in
    controlled drugs);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} section 48 (trafficking in restricted drugs);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} section 50.2 (possession of property obtained by
    trafficking in restricted drugs); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} section 50.3 (laundering proceeds of trafficking in
    restricted drugs).

- **3** An offence under any of the following provisions of the
  [Controlled Drugs and Substances Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8) that was
  prosecuted by way of indictment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 5 (trafficking);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 6 (importing and exporting);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 7 (production).

  - [(d) and (e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 32, s.
    57\]]{.Repealed}

- **4** An offence under any of the following provisions of the
  [Cannabis Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5) that was prosecuted by way of
  indictment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 9 (distribution and possession for purpose
    of distributing);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 10 (selling and possession for purpose of
    selling);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 11 (importing and exporting and possession
    for purpose of exporting);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} section 12 (production);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} section 13 (possession, etc., for use in production
    or distribution of illicit cannabis); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} section 14 (use of young person).

- **5** The offence of conspiring, as provided by paragraph 465(1)(c) of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), to commit any of the offences
  referred to in items 1 to 4 of this schedule that was prosecuted by
  way of indictment.

::: HistoricalNote

- 2001, c. 32, s. 57
- 2011, c. 11, s. 9
- 2018, c. 16, s. 172

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 1995, c. 42, s. 88

  - ###### Full parole {#full-parole .MarginalNote}

    [88]{.lawlabel} Sections 120.1, 120.2 and 120.3 of the [Corrections
    and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by
    section 34 of this Act, only apply in respect of an offender who
    receives an additional sentence after the coming into force of those
    sections.

- --- 1995, c. 42, s. 90

  - ###### Detention {#detention .MarginalNote}

    - [90]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 129, 130 and 132 of the
      [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as
      enacted by sections 44, 45 and 47, respectively, of this Act,
      apply to every offender sentenced in respect of an offence
      referred to in any of those provisions regardless of the day on
      which the offender was sentenced, committed or transferred to
      penitentiary.

    - ###### Referral to Board {#referral-to-board .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Service may, within thirty days after the
      coming into force of paragraphs 129(2)(a) and (b) of the
      [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as
      enacted by subsection 44(2) of this Act, refer to the Board the
      case of an offender where the Service is of the opinion set out in
      subparagraph 129(2)(a)(ii) of the [Corrections and Conditional
      Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by subsection 44(2) of
      this Act, even if the referral takes place later than six months
      before the day on which the offender is entitled to be released on
      statutory release.

    - ###### Idem {#idem .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may, within thirty days after
      the coming into force of subsection 129(3) of the [Corrections and
      Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by
      subsection 44(3) of this Act, refer the case of an offender who is
      serving a sentence of two years or more to the Chairperson of the
      Board for review under that subsection later than six months
      before the day on which the offender is entitled to be released on
      statutory release, where the Commissioner believes on reasonable
      grounds that the offender is likely, before the expiration of the
      sentence according to law, to commit a sexual offence involving a
      child.

- --- 1995, c. 42, s. 91

  - ###### Revocation of parole or statutory release {#revocation-of-parole-or-statutory-release .MarginalNote}

    [91]{.lawlabel} The revocation of parole or statutory release
    provided by subsection 135(9.1) of the [Corrections and Conditional
    Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by subsection 50(7) of
    this Act, only applies where the additional sentence is imposed
    after the coming into force of subsection 135(9.1) of that Act.

- --- 1995, c. 42, s 92

  - ###### Interruption of parole or statutory release {#interruption-of-parole-or-statutory-release .MarginalNote}

    - [92]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the sentence of an offender
      who is on parole or statutory release is interrupted pursuant to
      subsection 139(2) of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
      Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as it read immediately before the coming
      into force of section 54 of this Act, that sentence is not resumed
      until the later sentence expires or until the parole or statutory
      release is terminated or revoked.

    - ###### Idem {#idem-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the sentence of an offender who is on
      parole or statutory release is interrupted pursuant to subsection
      139(2) of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
      Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as it read immediately before the coming
      into force of section 54 of this Act, and the parole or statutory
      release is terminated or revoked, the offender shall serve the
      total of

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the unexpired portion of the sentence the
        offender was serving while on parole or statutory release, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the unexpired portion of any later sentence.

- --- 2001, c. 41, s. 94, as amended by 2011, c. 11, s. 13

  - ###### Transitional provision {#transitional-provision .MarginalNote}

    - [94]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions apply to
      an offender regardless of the day on which the offender was
      sentenced, committed or transferred to penitentiary:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2011, c. 11, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} Schedule I to the Act as amended by sections 91
        to 93.

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2011, c. 11, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

- --- 2011, c. 11, s. 10

  - ###### Application {#application .MarginalNote}

    - [10]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
      accelerated parole review process set out in sections 125 to 126.1
      of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
      Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as those sections read on the day before
      the day on which section 5 comes into force, does not apply, as of
      that day, to offenders who were sentenced, committed or
      transferred to penitentiary, whether the sentencing, committal or
      transfer occurs before, on or after the day of that coming into
      force.

    - ###### Restriction {#restriction .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the repeal of sections 125
      to 126.1 of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
      Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) does not affect the validity of a direction
      made under those sections before the day on which section 5 comes
      into force.

- --- 2012, c. 1, s. 105

  - ###### Recalculation of statutory release date {#recalculation-of-statutory-release-date .MarginalNote}

    [105]{.lawlabel} Subsection 127(5.1) of the [Corrections and
    Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by section
    81, applies only in respect of an offender who is on parole or
    statutory release and who receives an additional sentence for an
    offence under an Act of Parliament on or after the day on which this
    section comes into force.

- --- 2012, c. 1, s. 106

  - ###### Detention {#detention-1 .MarginalNote}

    [106]{.lawlabel} Subparagraph 129(2)(a)(ii) of the [Corrections and
    Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) and subparagraphs
    (a)(iv.1) and (vii.1) of the definition [[sexual offence involving a
    child]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection 129(9) of that Act, as
    enacted by section 84, apply in respect of an offender who is
    sentenced in respect of an offence referred to in any of those
    subparagraphs, even if they were sentenced, committed or transferred
    to a penitentiary before the day on which this section comes into
    force.

- --- 2012, c. 1, s. 107

  - ###### Automatic suspension, cancellation or revocation {#automatic-suspension-cancellation-or-revocation .MarginalNote}

    [107]{.lawlabel} Subsections 135(1.1) to (3.1), (6.2) to (6.4),
    (9.1) and (9.2) of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
    Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted or amended by section 89, apply
    only in respect of an offender who receives an additional sentence
    on or after the day on which this section comes into force.

- --- 2012, c. 19, s. 528

  - ###### Hearings {#hearings .MarginalNote}

    [528]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 140(1)(d) of the [Corrections and
    Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as enacted by section
    527, applies only in respect of a review of the case of an offender
    begun on or after the day on which this section comes into force.

- --- 2014, c. 25, s. 45.1

  - ###### Review {#review .MarginalNote}

    - [45.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within five years after this
      section comes into force, a comprehensive review of the provisions
      and operation of this Act shall be undertaken by such committee of
      the House of Commons as may be designated or established by the
      House for that purpose.

    - ###### Report {#report .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1)
      shall, within a year after a review is undertaken pursuant to that
      subsection or within such further time as the House may authorize,
      submit a report on the review to the Speaker of the House,
      including a statement of any changes the committee recommends.

- --- 2015, c. 11, s. 7

  - ###### Additional sentence {#additional-sentence .MarginalNote}

    - [7]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 123(5.01) and (5.2) of
      the [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6),
      as enacted by section 2 --- and subsection 131(1.1) of the Act, as
      enacted by section 3 --- apply in respect of an offender who
      receives an additional sentence for an offence under an Act of
      Parliament on or after the day on which this section comes into
      force.

    - ###### Additional sentence --- on parole or statutory release {#additional-sentence-on-parole-or-statutory-release .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 123(5.01) and (5.2) of the
      [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as
      enacted by section 2 --- and subsection 131(1.1) of the Act, as
      enacted by section 3 --- apply in respect of an offender who is on
      parole or statutory release and who receives an additional
      sentence for an offence under an Act of Parliament on or after the
      day on which this section comes into force.

    - ###### First review --- section 122, 123 or 131 {#first-review-section-122-123-or-131 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 123(5.01) and (5.2) of the
      [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as
      enacted by section 2 --- and subsection 131(1.1) of the Act, as
      enacted by section 3 --- apply in respect of an offender even if
      they were sentenced, committed or transferred to a penitentiary
      before the day on which this section comes into force. However,
      they do not apply to the first review on or after that day, under
      section 122, 123 or 131, as the case may be.

- --- 2016, c. 3, s. 9.1

  - ###### Mature minors, advance requests and mental illness {#mature-minors-advance-requests-and-mental-illness .MarginalNote}

    - [9.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Justice and the
      Minister of Health must, no later than 180 days after the day on
      which this Act receives royal assent, initiate one or more
      independent reviews of issues relating to requests by mature
      minors for medical assistance in dying, to advance requests and to
      requests where mental illness is the sole underlying medical
      condition.

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Justice and the Minister of
      Health must, no later than two years after the day on which a
      review is initiated, cause one or more reports on the review,
      including any findings or recommendations resulting from it, to be
      laid before each House of Parliament.

- --- 2016, c. 3, s. 10

  - ###### Review by committee {#review-by-committee .MarginalNote}

    - [10]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} At the start of the fifth year
      after the day on which this Act receives royal assent, the
      provisions enacted by this Act are to be referred to the committee
      of the Senate, of the House of Commons or of both Houses of
      Parliament that may be designated or established for the purpose
      of reviewing the provisions.

    - ###### Report {#report-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee to which the provisions are
      referred is to review them and the state of palliative care in
      Canada and submit a report to the House or Houses of Parliament of
      which it is a committee, including a statement setting out any
      changes to the provisions that the committee recommends.

- --- 2019, c. 27, s. 38

  - ###### Words and expressions {#words-and-expressions .MarginalNote}

    [38]{.lawlabel} Unless the context otherwise requires, words and
    expressions used in sections 39 and 40 have the same meaning as in
    subsection 2(1) or 99(1) of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
    Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6).

- --- 2019, c. 27, s. 39

  - ###### Inmate is in administrative segregation {#inmate-is-in-administrative-segregation .MarginalNote}

    [39]{.lawlabel} If an inmate is in administrative segregation
    immediately before the coming into force of section 10, on the day
    on which that section comes into force that inmate is deemed to have
    been authorized to be transferred to a structured intervention unit
    under an authorization given under subsection 29.01(1) of the
    [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), as
    enacted by section 7.

- --- 2019, c. 27, s. 40

  - ###### Sanction under paragraph 44(1)(f) {#sanction-under-paragraph-441f .MarginalNote}

    [40]{.lawlabel} An inmate who is subject to a disciplinary sanction
    referred to in paragraph 44(1)(f) of the [Corrections and
    Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) immediately before the
    coming into force of section 11 ceases to be subject to that
    sanction on the day on which that section comes into force.

- --- 2019, c. 27, s. 40.1

  - ###### Review by committee {#review-by-committee-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [40.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} At the start of the fifth year
      after the day on which this section comes into force, a
      comprehensive review of the provisions enacted by this Act must be
      undertaken by the committee of the Senate, of the House of Commons
      or of both Houses of Parliament that may be designated or
      established for that purpose.

    - ###### Report to Parliament {#report-to-parliament .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1) must,
      within one year after the review is undertaken under that
      subsection, submit a report to the House or Houses of Parliament
      of which it is a committee, including a statement setting out any
      changes to the provisions that the committee recommends.

- --- 2024, c. 17, s. 391

  - [391]{.lawlabel} On the coming into force of section 389, any
    arrangement entered into under section 94.3 of [Corrections and
    Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) that is still in effect
    is terminated and any designation made under subsection 94.4(1) of
    that Act is revoked.
:::
::::

:::: section
::: ScheduleNIF
## AMENDMENTS NOT IN FORCE {#amendments-not-in-force .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2015, c. 11, s. 6

  - - [6]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraph 142(1)(b)(iii) of the
      Act is repealed.

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subparagraphs 142(1)(b)(v) and (vi) of the Act
      are repealed.

    - [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 142(1)(b) of the Act is amended by
      striking out "and" at the end of subparagraph (viii) and by adding
      the following after that subparagraph:

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - [(viii.1)]{.lawlabel} information pertaining to the offender's
        correctional plan, including information regarding the
        offender's progress towards meeting the objectives of the plan,
        and
      :::
      ::::

    - [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 142(1) of the Act is amended by
      striking out "and" at the end of paragraph (a), by adding "and" at
      the end of paragraph (b) and by adding the following after
      paragraph (b):

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall disclose to the victim any of the
        following information about the offender, if, in the
        Chairperson's opinion, the disclosure would not have a negative
        impact on the safety of the public:

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date, if any, on which the offender is to
          be released on unescorted temporary absence, escorted
          temporary absence if the Board has approved the absence as
          required by subsection 746.1(2) of the [Criminal
          Code](/eng/acts/C-46), parole or statutory release,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any conditions attached to the offender's
          unescorted temporary absence, parole or statutory release and
          the reasons for any unescorted temporary absence, and

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the destination of the offender when
          released on unescorted temporary absence, parole or statutory
          release and whether the offender will be in the vicinity of
          the victim while travelling to that destination.
      :::
      ::::

    - [(5)]{.lawlabel} Section 142 of the Act is amended by adding the
      following after subsection (1):

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - ###### Timing of disclosure {#timing-of-disclosure .MarginalNote}

        [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall disclose the
        information referred to in paragraph (1)(c) at least 14 days,
        where practicable, before the offender in question is to be
        released.

      - ###### Continuing duty to disclose {#continuing-duty-to-disclose .MarginalNote}

        [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If a victim makes a request to the
        Chairperson under subsection (1) in respect of the information
        relating to an offender and, subsequent to that initial request,
        there are changes to that information, the Chairperson shall, in
        accordance with that subsection, disclose any such changes to
        the victim, unless the victim notifies the Chairperson that he
        or she does not wish to be so informed.
      :::
      ::::

- --- 2015, c. 13, s. 58

  - ###### Bill C-479 {#bill-c-479 .MarginalNote}

    [58]{.lawlabel} If Bill C-479, introduced in the 1st session of the
    41st Parliament and entitled An Act to bring Fairness for the
    Victims of Violent Offenders, receives royal assent, then, on the
    first day on which both subsection 6(3) of that Act and subsection
    46(4) of this Act are in force, subsection 142(1.1) of the
    [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6) is
    replaced by the following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Timing of disclosure {#timing-of-disclosure-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall disclose the information
      referred to in paragraph (1)(c) before the day on which the
      offender is released and, unless it is not practicable to do so,
      the Chairperson shall disclose it at least 14 days before that
      day.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2024, c. 17, s. 385(2)

  - - [385]{.lawlabel} [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 5(f) of the Act is
      repealed.

- --- 2024, c. 17, s. 387

  - [387]{.lawlabel} Section 58.1 of the Act is repealed.

- --- 2024, c. 17, s. 389

  - [389]{.lawlabel} The heading before section 94.1 and sections 94.1
    to 94.8 of the Act are repealed.

- --- 2024, c. 23, par. 12(b)

  - ###### Replacement of "child pornography" {#replacement-of-child-pornography .MarginalNote}

    [12]{.lawlabel} Every reference to "child pornography" is replaced
    by a reference to "child sexual abuse and exploitation material" in
    the following provisions:

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the [Corrections and Conditional Release
      Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6),

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} subparagraph (a)(iv.1) of the definition
        [[sexual offence involving a child]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in
        subsection 129(9), and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 1(j.1) of Schedule I;
